# Marking sheet

Answers: 574.

## Answer 8870

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A screening is advertised to begin at 19:30 on 18 November 2025. Someone has paid for a reserved seat but has not arrived when the film starts. Until what exact time must the society hold that seat, and when do the doors normally open?

**Expected answer:** The reserved seat must be held until 19:40, which is 10 minutes after the advertised 19:30 start. Doors normally open at 19:00, which is 30 minutes before the advertised start. The seat-holding deadline is not the same as the door-opening time.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give both 19:40 for the reserved-seat deadline and 19:00 for door opening. It is wrong if it gives 19:30 as the seat deadline, 19:00 as both times, or 20:00 by adding the 30-minute door-opening interval after the start.

**The answer to mark:**

The society must hold the seat until 19:40, which is 10 minutes after the advertised 19:30 start (film-screenings.md, section 3.4). Doors normally open at 19:00, 30 minutes before the advertised start (film-screenings.md, section 3.3).

No change in film-changes.md affects these sections.

## Answer 1242

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: At a screening on 14 November 2025, a visitor makes a serious threat against another attendee. The visitor has not previously been warned about their behaviour. Must the duty volunteer issue a warning before requiring the visitor to leave?

**Expected answer:** No. Serious threats can result in immediate removal without a warning. Although the ordinary disruption procedure allows a volunteer to ask someone to stop and then require them to leave if they refuse, the serious-threat exception permits immediate removal.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say that no prior warning is required for a serious threat and distinguish that exception from the ordinary stop-and-refuse procedure. It is wrong if it insists that the visitor must first receive a warning or refuse an instruction before removal is permitted.

**The answer to mark:**

**No.** The duty volunteer does not have to warn the visitor first. A serious threat may lead to immediate removal without a warning.

**Rule:** film-conduct.md, section 2.3 says "Serious threats may result in immediate removal without a warning." Section 1.2 prohibits threats. Section 2.2 allows a person to be required to leave.

No change in the changes register replaces these sections. The conduct document was effective from 1 April 2025, so it applies on 14 November 2025.

## Answer 1427

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: Two adults have accepted membership applications. The treasurer receives the first person's £24 payment on 30 June 2025 and the second person's payment on 2 July 2025. How much should each person pay for annual adult membership?

**Expected answer:** The first person pays £24 because the treasurer received payment before 1 July 2025. The second person pays £28 because the revised adult membership fee took effect on 1 July 2025. The payment receipt date, not the application acceptance date, determines which fee applies.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give £24 for 30 June 2025 and £28 for 2 July 2025, explaining that the payment date controls. It is wrong if it charges both £24 using the original fee schedule, both £28 using the latest rate without considering dates, or £20 using the archived schedule.

**The answer to mark:**

**First person: £24. Second person: £28.**

- The fee depends on the date the payment is received (film-changes.md, section 1.3). The 16 June 2025 decision replaces film-fees.md section 1.1 (film-changes.md, sections 1.1–1.2). From 1 July 2025, annual adult membership costs £28 instead of £24. Payments received before 1 July 2025 keep the old fee (film-changes.md, section 1.4).
- The first person's payment was received on 30 June 2025, which is before 1 July, so the old £24 fee applies.
- The second person's payment was received on 2 July 2025, which is after 1 July, so the new £28 fee applies.

A payment counts as received when cash is handed over or a transfer clears (film-fees.md, section 4.5). I took the dates you gave as those dates. If the second person's payment was a bank transfer, 2 July should be the date it cleared.

## Answer 9856

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: Four trustees want to attend a committee meeting on 15 November 2026, but one proposes joining by video call from home while the other three attend in person. Do the rules allow a remote trustee to count towards the required quorum, and would that make this meeting quorate?

**Expected answer:** The papers do not say whether trustees may attend remotely or whether remote attendance counts towards the quorum. They require four trustees for a quorum but explicitly leave remote attendance unspecified. Consequently, the papers alone cannot establish whether three trustees attending in person plus one joining by video call would constitute a valid quorum.

**Correct requires:** The answer must identify four as the quorum, state that remote attendance is not addressed and decline to decide whether the proposed meeting is quorate. It is wrong if it automatically counts the video participant and declares a quorum of four, or automatically excludes that participant and declares only three without acknowledging the missing remote-attendance rule.

**The answer to mark:**

The documents don't say, so I can't give a yes or no. The rules don't specify whether trustees may attend remotely (hall-trustees.md, section 6.3). They also don't say whether a remote attendee counts toward the quorum. The changes document has nothing on this either.

Whether the meeting is quorate depends on whether the video-call trustee counts as present:
- **If the remote trustee counts:** four trustees are present, which meets the quorum of four (hall-trustees.md, section 4.2).
- **If the remote trustee does not count:** only three are present, which is below the quorum of four (hall-trustees.md, section 4.2).

## Answer 5701

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: A registered village group has exactly nine local members and supplies that count when booking the Main Hall for two paid hours on 15 October 2025. The organiser requests the village group discount because the group is registered locally. Does it qualify, and what is the room-hire charge without the discount?

**Expected answer:** No, the group does not qualify for the village group discount because the rules require at least ten local members. Being registered is not sufficient when the group has only nine. On 15 October 2025, the Main Hall costs £24 per hour, so two paid hours cost £48 without a discount.

**Correct requires:** The answer must deny the discount because nine is below the ten-member minimum and give £48 as the room charge. It is wrong if it applies the original 20% discount and charges £38.40, applies the archived eight-member qualification and 15% discount to charge £40.80 at the governing hourly rate, or uses the later £28 hourly rate and charges £56.

**The answer to mark:**

No, it doesn't qualify. Without the discount, the room-hire charge is £48.

- **Discount:** A registered village group must have at least ten local members (hall-fees.md, section 3.2). Nine is below that. The 15 October 2025 event is before the 1 February 2026 change to section 3.1 (hall-changes.md, section 3), so the original section 3.1 applies, and its discount still depends on section 3.2.
- **Charge:** The Main Hall rate is £24 per hour (hall-fees.md, section 2.1). The increase to £28 applies only from 1 November 2025 (hall-changes.md, section 2), so it doesn't reach this event. Two paid hours, which is also the minimum booking (section 2.4), come to 2 × £24 = £48.

## Answer 7230

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A member aged 35 has an active annual membership but is suspended by the committee from 1 November through 20 November 2025. They arrive at a public screening on 8 November 2025 and ask to pay the usual member admission price. Are they entitled to the discount, and what admission price applies instead?

**Expected answer:** No, the suspended member is not entitled to the member discount during the suspension. Suspended members cannot claim member prices, even when their annual membership would otherwise be active. The standard admission price on 8 November 2025 is £7 rather than the £4 active-member price.

**Correct requires:** The answer must deny the discount and give £7 as the applicable standard admission charge, contrasting it with the £4 member price. It is wrong if it charges £4 simply because the annual membership has not expired, £5 by confusing the child ticket band or later member rate, or says suspension permanently ends the membership.

**The answer to mark:**

**No, they aren't entitled to the member price. Standard admission of £7 applies.**

- **Suspension removes member prices.** `film-membership.md` 5.4 says "A suspended member cannot vote, book equipment or claim member prices." Conduct rule 4.4 sends membership rights during suspension to that file.
- **The suspension covers the visit.** It runs 1–20 November 2025, so 8 November falls inside it. It is also within the 30-day maximum in `film-conduct.md` 4.1. The suspension doesn't end the membership, so the member can still attend as a paying visitor.
- **Their age doesn't change the price.** At 35 they fall in none of the child rates. Those are under 12 at £3 and 12–15 at £5 (`film-fees.md` 2.3 and 2.4).
- **Standard admission is £7.** `film-fees.md` 2.1 sets this "per screening". Section 2.2 gives members with *active* membership £4. The member would normally get that rate, but suspension means they can't claim it here.
- **The 2026 change doesn't apply.** `film-changes.md` section 3 raises the member price to £5, but only from 1 February 2026. It is set by screening date, so it doesn't affect 8 November 2025. Either way, it wouldn't help a suspended member.

This assumes the suspension was made validly under `film-conduct.md` section 4. That requires a majority vote of uninvolved committee members and a written decision stating the reason and dates. I can't tell from the documents whether that happened. Admission is also subject to the capacity rules in `film-screenings.md` (`film-fees.md` 2.6). I didn't check those.

## Answer 9613

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: A hirer books the Meeting Room on 6 September 2025 and asks for one portable speaker and three sets of folding display boards. Ignoring room hire and deposits, what is the total equipment charge?

**Expected answer:** The total equipment charge is £25. The portable speaker costs £10 per booking and folding display boards cost £5 per set per booking. Three sets cost £15, so the speaker and boards together cost £10 + £15 = £25.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give £25 and show £10 + (3 × £5). It is wrong if it charges £15 by treating all three sets of boards as one set, £13 by using the archived speaker and board prices as one-time charges, or £20 by charging for only two sets.

**The answer to mark:**

The total equipment charge is £25. That is £10 for the portable speaker plus £15 for three sets of display boards at £5 each. Both charges are per booking (hall-fees.md, sections 4.2 and 4.3).

hall-changes.md doesn't replace either section. The 1 April 2025 start in hall-fees.md section 1.2 also covers a 6 September 2025 booking.

**Expected answer:** The reserved seat must be held until 19:40, which is 10 minutes after the advertised 19:30 start. Doors normally open at 19:00, which is 30 minutes before the advertised start. The seat-holding deadline is not the same as the door-opening time.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give both 19:40 for the reserved-seat deadline and 19:00 for door opening. It is wrong if it gives 19:30 as the seat deadline, 19:00 as both times, or 20:00 by adding the 30-minute door-opening interval after the start.

**The answer to mark:**

The society must hold the seat until **19:40** on 18 November 2025. Doors normally open at **19:00**.

- **Seat hold:** Section 3.4 of `film-screenings.md` says reserved seats are held until 10 minutes after the advertised start. The advertised start is 19:30, so the hold runs to 19:40.
- **Doors:** Section 3.3 says doors open 30 minutes before the advertised start, which is 19:00.

None of the dated amendments in `film-changes.md` touch these sections. The only screening-rule change is to the maximum audience (3.1), which doesn't affect this question.

Section 3.5 adds that late arrivals may enter only during a suitable break. That is a separate rule from the seat hold.

## Answer 1297

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: An organiser plans a children's event in the Meeting Room on 19 October 2025. There will be 16 children aged 8 to 11 and five adults, including organisers. The organiser wants to know whether the attendance is permitted and whether the children may be left without adult supervision while the adults prepare another room. What do the rules require?

**Expected answer:** The attendance is permitted because the Meeting Room capacity is 24 people and the total is 21, including the adults and children. However, children under 12 must remain under adult supervision. The organiser therefore cannot leave the children unsupervised while the adults prepare another room.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give a total attendance of 21 against the capacity of 24, permit the attendance and require continuing adult supervision. It is wrong if it rejects the event by counting children and adults against separate capacity allowances, counts only the five adults, or says that being below capacity permits the 16 children to remain unsupervised.

**The answer to mark:**

**Attendance is permitted, but the children may not be left without adult supervision.**

**Attendance**
- The Meeting Room maximum is 24 people (hall-bookings.md §4.2).
- Capacity includes organisers and children (§4.4).
- 16 children plus 5 adults is 21, which is under the limit.
- No entry in hall-changes.md replaces these sections.

**Supervision**
- Children under 12 must remain under adult supervision (hall-safety.md §3.3).
- Children aged 8 to 11 are all under 12, so the rule covers every child.
- The rules make no exception for adults preparing another room. At least one adult must stay supervising the children.
- Separately, at least one responsible adult must remain near the main entrance (§3.4).
- The rules don't set an adult-to-child ratio. They also don't say whether the adult at the entrance can be the one supervising the children.

## Answer 4722

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A parent brings their 9-year-old child to a public screening on 16 August 2025 and accompanies them throughout. A volunteer has found an old price list showing that children under 12 pay £2. What is the correct ticket price for the child?

**Expected answer:** The correct ticket price is £3. The £2 price appears in the archived schedule that was superseded on 1 March 2025. The governing schedule charges accompanied children under 12 £3, and no later change alters that concession.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give £3, noting that the child is under 12 and accompanied by an adult. It is wrong if it gives £2 from the archived schedule, £5 for the 12–15 age group, or £7 for standard admission while overlooking the applicable child concession.

**The answer to mark:**

The child's ticket costs £3. Children under 12 pay £3 when accompanied by an adult, and the 9-year-old is accompanied throughout (film-fees.md, section 2.3). The £2 price list is the superseded schedule, which ended on 28 February 2025 and can't be used for current charges (film-fees-archive.md, sections 2.3 and 5.1). No committee change affects child tickets.

## Answer 8152

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: Four trustees want to attend a committee meeting on 15 November 2026, but one proposes joining by video call from home while the other three attend in person. Do the rules allow a remote trustee to count towards the required quorum, and would that make this meeting quorate?

**Expected answer:** The papers do not say whether trustees may attend remotely or whether remote attendance counts towards the quorum. They require four trustees for a quorum but explicitly leave remote attendance unspecified. Consequently, the papers alone cannot establish whether three trustees attending in person plus one joining by video call would constitute a valid quorum.

**Correct requires:** The answer must identify four as the quorum, state that remote attendance is not addressed and decline to decide whether the proposed meeting is quorate. It is wrong if it automatically counts the video participant and declares a quorum of four, or automatically excludes that participant and declares only three without acknowledging the missing remote-attendance rule.

**The answer to mark:**

The rules don't say whether a remote trustee counts towards quorum, so they don't settle whether the meeting is quorate.

Four trustees form a quorum (hall-trustees.md, section 4.2). The rules also say they "do not specify whether trustees may attend remotely" (hall-trustees.md, section 6.3), and nothing in the changes register covers this.

- **Remote trustee counts:** the meeting would have four and be quorate.
- **Remote trustee doesn't count:** only three would be present and the meeting would not be quorate.

The committee would have to decide which applies, or the three in-person trustees could avoid the question by getting a fourth trustee to attend in person.

## Answer 1932

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: Two adults have accepted membership applications. The treasurer receives the first person's £24 payment on 30 June 2025 and the second person's payment on 2 July 2025. How much should each person pay for annual adult membership?

**Expected answer:** The first person pays £24 because the treasurer received payment before 1 July 2025. The second person pays £28 because the revised adult membership fee took effect on 1 July 2025. The payment receipt date, not the application acceptance date, determines which fee applies.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give £24 for 30 June 2025 and £28 for 2 July 2025, explaining that the payment date controls. It is wrong if it charges both £24 using the original fee schedule, both £28 using the latest rate without considering dates, or £20 using the archived schedule.

**The answer to mark:**

**The first person pays £24 and the second pays £28.**

- **Fee rule:** Annual adult membership is £24 under `film-fees.md` §1.1. The committee decision of 16 June 2025 (`film-changes.md` §1) raised it to £28 from 1 July 2025.
- **Which fee applies:** The date the membership payment is received sets the fee (§1.3). Payments received before 1 July 2025 keep the old fee (§1.4).
- **First person:** Their payment was received on 30 June 2025, before the change, so £24 is correct.
- **Second person:** Their payment was received on 2 July 2025, after the change, so the fee is £28.

The acceptance dates don't affect the fee, and neither does the fact that both applications were accepted. Payment is only due after acceptance (`film-membership.md` §2.5). Joining partway through the year doesn't reduce the fee either (`film-fees.md` §1.3).

## Answer 7630

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A member aged 35 has an active annual membership but is suspended by the committee from 1 November through 20 November 2025. They arrive at a public screening on 8 November 2025 and ask to pay the usual member admission price. Are they entitled to the discount, and what admission price applies instead?

**Expected answer:** No, the suspended member is not entitled to the member discount during the suspension. Suspended members cannot claim member prices, even when their annual membership would otherwise be active. The standard admission price on 8 November 2025 is £7 rather than the £4 active-member price.

**Correct requires:** The answer must deny the discount and give £7 as the applicable standard admission charge, contrasting it with the £4 member price. It is wrong if it charges £4 simply because the annual membership has not expired, £5 by confusing the child ticket band or later member rate, or says suspension permanently ends the membership.

**The answer to mark:**

**No.** The member is suspended on 8 November 2025, so they can't claim the member price. They pay standard admission of **£7**.

- **Rule on member prices:** film-membership.md §5.4 says a suspended member cannot claim member prices. The suspension runs 1–20 November 2025 and covers 8 November. Film-conduct.md §4.4 sends the question of rights during suspension to film-membership.md.
- **Rule on the price:** film-fees.md §2.1 sets standard admission at £7 per screening.
- **Change check:** film-changes.md §3 replaces the £4 member price in film-fees.md §2.2 with £5, but only from 1 February 2026. It doesn't touch the £7 standard price, and it doesn't apply to a screening on 8 November 2025.

The member's age, 35, doesn't affect the price. The age-based prices in film-fees.md §2.3 and §2.4 cover only children under 16.

## Answer 7146

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: A band is hired for a Saturday evening event on 4 April 2026. Their contract says amplified music will continue until 21:45. The hirer points out that the general hall rules allow Friday and Saturday amplified music until 22:00. May the band play until 21:45?

**Expected answer:** No. From 1 April 2026, amplified music on Friday and Saturday must stop by 21:30. Because the music is being played on 4 April 2026, the revised cutoff applies, and playing until 21:45 would exceed it by 15 minutes.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say no, give 21:30 as the controlling cutoff and identify the proposed 15-minute overrun. It is wrong if it permits 21:45 based on the original 22:00 cutoff, or treats the building's 23:00 Saturday closing time as permission for amplified music until 23:00.

**The answer to mark:**

No. Amplified music on a Friday or Saturday must stop by 21:30 (hall-changes.md, section 5). That change took effect on 1 April 2026 and replaced the 22:00 cutoff in hall-conduct.md section 4.1. It applies by the date the music is played, which is 4 April 2026. The band's 21:45 finish runs 15 minutes past the cutoff.

## Answer 3962

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: Two adults have accepted membership applications. The treasurer receives the first person's £24 payment on 30 June 2025 and the second person's payment on 2 July 2025. How much should each person pay for annual adult membership?

**Expected answer:** The first person pays £24 because the treasurer received payment before 1 July 2025. The second person pays £28 because the revised adult membership fee took effect on 1 July 2025. The payment receipt date, not the application acceptance date, determines which fee applies.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give £24 for 30 June 2025 and £28 for 2 July 2025, explaining that the payment date controls. It is wrong if it charges both £24 using the original fee schedule, both £28 using the latest rate without considering dates, or £20 using the archived schedule.

**The answer to mark:**

Under the Fenwray Film Society rules:

- **First person: £24.** Their payment is received on 30 June 2025, which is before 1 July 2025. The old fee is kept (film-changes.md 1.4).
- **Second person: £28.** Their payment is received on 2 July 2025, which is after the change. From 1 July 2025 annual adult membership costs £28 instead of £24 (film-changes.md 1.2).

The fee depends on the date the payment is received, not the date of acceptance or application (film-changes.md 1.3). A payment counts as received when cash is handed over or a transfer clears (film-fees.md 4.5). The original fee of £24 is in film-fees.md 1.1, and the 16 June 2025 decision replaces it.

The documents don't say what happens if the second person's payment was made earlier but only cleared on 2 July. I've taken 2 July as the date of receipt, as you described.

## Answer 5093

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: A registered village group has exactly nine local members and supplies that count when booking the Main Hall for two paid hours on 15 October 2025. The organiser requests the village group discount because the group is registered locally. Does it qualify, and what is the room-hire charge without the discount?

**Expected answer:** No, the group does not qualify for the village group discount because the rules require at least ten local members. Being registered is not sufficient when the group has only nine. On 15 October 2025, the Main Hall costs £24 per hour, so two paid hours cost £48 without a discount.

**Correct requires:** The answer must deny the discount because nine is below the ten-member minimum and give £48 as the room charge. It is wrong if it applies the original 20% discount and charges £38.40, applies the archived eight-member qualification and 15% discount to charge £40.80 at the governing hourly rate, or uses the later £28 hourly rate and charges £56.

**The answer to mark:**

**The group doesn't qualify, and the room-hire charge is £48.**

- **Discount:** A registered village group must have at least ten local members (`hall-fees.md` §3.2). This group has nine, so it falls short. Being registered locally isn't enough on its own.
- **Rate:** The committee raised the Main Hall rate from £24 to £28 an hour, but only for events starting on or after 1 November 2025 (`hall-changes.md` §2). An event on 15 October 2025 is before that date, so the original £24 an hour applies (`hall-fees.md` §2.1).
- **Charge:** Two paid hours at £24 an hour is £48. Two hours is also the minimum paid booking (§2.4).

The £48 covers room hire only. Any kitchen use (£8), equipment or deposits (£30 booking deposit, which counts towards the hire charge) would be extra.

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: A band is hired for a Saturday evening event on 4 April 2026. Their contract says amplified music will continue until 21:45. **Expected answer:** No. From 1 April 2026, amplified music on Friday and Saturday must stop by 21:30. Because the music is being played on 4 April 2026, the revised cutoff applies, and playing until 21:45 would exceed it by 15 minutes.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say no, give 21:30 as the controlling cutoff and identify the proposed 15-minute overrun. It is wrong if it permits 21:45 based on the original 22:00 cutoff, or treats the building's 23:00 Saturday closing time as permission for amplified music until 23:00.

**The answer to mark:**

No. Under the hall rules, the band must stop by 21:30, so 21:45 is not allowed.

- `hall-conduct.md` section 4.1 originally set the Friday and Saturday amplified music cutoff at 22:00. That is the rule the hirer is relying on.
- `hall-changes.md` section 5 replaced 4.1 with a 21:30 cutoff, effective 1 April 2026 (decided 20 March 2026).
- The controlling date is the date the music is played (5.3). That is 4 April 2026, a Saturday, which is after the effective date. The replacement therefore applies.

The documents I checked have no provision that lets a hire contract set a later cutoff than the rules. The 22:00 figure the hirer cites is no longer current for that date.

I can't say whether the hall would grant an exception. These documents don't provide for one.

## Answer 1639

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A member aged 35 has an active annual membership but is suspended by the committee from 1 November through 20 November 2025. They arrive at a public screening on 8 November 2025 and ask to pay the usual member admission price. Are they entitled to the discount, and what admission price applies instead?

**Expected answer:** No, the suspended member is not entitled to the member discount during the suspension. Suspended members cannot claim member prices, even when their annual membership would otherwise be active. The standard admission price on 8 November 2025 is £7 rather than the £4 active-member price.

**Correct requires:** The answer must deny the discount and give £7 as the applicable standard admission charge, contrasting it with the £4 member price. It is wrong if it charges £4 simply because the annual membership has not expired, £5 by confusing the child ticket band or later member rate, or says suspension permanently ends the membership.

**The answer to mark:**

No. A suspended member cannot claim member prices (film-membership.md, section 5.4), so the £5 member admission in force on 8 November 2025 does not apply. Their admission is the standard £7 per screening (film-fees.md, section 2.1). Nothing in section 2 gives a 35-year-old a concession; the child rates in 2.3 and 2.4 don't apply.

The £5 member price comes from the 19 January 2026 decision (film-changes.md, section 3). It applies only from 1 February 2026, so it is not relevant to a November 2025 screening.

## Answer 7973

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: Four trustees want to attend a committee meeting on 15 November 2026, but one proposes joining by video call from home while the other three attend in person. Do the rules allow a remote trustee to count towards the required quorum, and would that make this meeting quorate?

**Expected answer:** The papers do not say whether trustees may attend remotely or whether remote attendance counts towards the quorum. They require four trustees for a quorum but explicitly leave remote attendance unspecified. Consequently, the papers alone cannot establish whether three trustees attending in person plus one joining by video call would constitute a valid quorum.

**Correct requires:** The answer must identify four as the quorum, state that remote attendance is not addressed and decline to decide whether the proposed meeting is quorate. It is wrong if it automatically counts the video participant and declares a quorum of four, or automatically excludes that participant and declares only three without acknowledging the missing remote-attendance rule.

**The answer to mark:**

The rules don't say whether a remote trustee counts towards quorum, so they don't settle the question.

- **What the rules say:** Four trustees form a quorum (hall-trustees.md, 4.2). The rules "do not specify whether trustees may attend remotely" (hall-trustees.md, 6.3). Nothing in the changes register alters either section (hall-changes.md).
- **If the video-call trustee counts:** four trustees are present, so the meeting would be quorate.
- **If the video-call trustee doesn't count:** only three are present, so the meeting would not be quorate.

The rules give no basis for choosing between these. The meeting is certainly quorate only if all four trustees attend in person.

## Answer 2105

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: A registered village group has exactly nine local members and supplies that count when booking the Main Hall for two paid hours on 15 October 2025. The organiser requests the village group discount because the group is registered locally. Does it qualify, and what is the room-hire charge without the discount?

**Expected answer:** No, the group does not qualify for the village group discount because the rules require at least ten local members. Being registered is not sufficient when the group has only nine. On 15 October 2025, the Main Hall costs £24 per hour, so two paid hours cost £48 without a discount.

**Correct requires:** The answer must deny the discount because nine is below the ten-member minimum and give £48 as the room charge. It is wrong if it applies the original 20% discount and charges £38.40, applies the archived eight-member qualification and 15% discount to charge £40.80 at the governing hourly rate, or uses the later £28 hourly rate and charges £56.

**The answer to mark:**

No. The group has nine local members and the minimum is ten (hall-fees.md, section 3.2). Section 3.1 gives the discount only to registered village groups, and registration alone doesn't meet that minimum.

Without the discount, the room-hire charge is £48: two hours at £24 per hour (hall-fees.md, sections 2.1 and 2.4). The 15 October 2025 event falls before the 1 November 2025 effective date of the £28 rate, so the changes register doesn't apply (hall-changes.md, section 2).

## Answer 4337

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: A hirer books the Meeting Room on 6 September 2025 and asks for one portable speaker and three sets of folding display boards. Ignoring room hire and deposits, what is the total equipment charge?

**Expected answer:** The total equipment charge is £25. The portable speaker costs £10 per booking and folding display boards cost £5 per set per booking. Three sets cost £15, so the speaker and boards together cost £10 + £15 = £25.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give £25 and show £10 + (3 × £5). It is wrong if it charges £15 by treating all three sets of boards as one set, £13 by using the archived speaker and board prices as one-time charges, or £20 by charging for only two sets.

**The answer to mark:**

£25. The speaker is £10 per booking and the display boards are £5 per set, so £10 + 3 × £5 = £25 (hall-fees.md, sections 4.2 and 4.3). The 6 September 2025 booking falls under these charges because they apply to bookings starting on or after 1 April 2025 (section 1.2).

## Answer 1558

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: At a screening on 14 November 2025, a visitor makes a serious threat against another attendee. The visitor has not previously been warned about their behaviour. Must the duty volunteer issue a warning before requiring the visitor to leave?

**Expected answer:** No. Serious threats can result in immediate removal without a warning. Although the ordinary disruption procedure allows a volunteer to ask someone to stop and then require them to leave if they refuse, the serious-threat exception permits immediate removal.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say that no prior warning is required for a serious threat and distinguish that exception from the ordinary stop-and-refuse procedure. It is wrong if it insists that the visitor must first receive a warning or refuse an instruction before removal is permitted.

**The answer to mark:**

**No.** The volunteer doesn't have to warn the visitor first.

Under `reference/film/film-conduct.md` §2.3, "Serious threats may result in immediate removal without a warning." Threats are prohibited under §1.2. A serious threat on 14 November 2025 therefore allows immediate removal, and it doesn't matter that the visitor had no earlier warning.

- **Warning route:** The ask-then-require-to-leave sequence in §2.1–2.2 applies to disruptive behaviour. It doesn't apply to serious threats.
- **Discretion:** §2.3 says "may", so removal is permitted, not compulsory.
- **Record:** The volunteer must record the incident within 48 hours (§2.4).
- **Refund:** Removal doesn't automatically give the visitor a refund right (§5.3). Eligibility is decided under `film-refunds.md` (§5.4).

I checked `film-changes.md` for amendments to the conduct rules and found none. The conduct rules were effective from 1 April 2025, so they applied on the incident date.

## Answer 4058

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: An organiser plans a children's event in the Meeting Room on 19 October 2025. There will be 16 children aged 8 to 11 and five adults, including organisers. The organiser wants to know whether the attendance is permitted and whether the children may be left without adult supervision while the adults prepare another room. What do the rules require?

**Expected answer:** The attendance is permitted because the Meeting Room capacity is 24 people and the total is 21, including the adults and children. However, children under 12 must remain under adult supervision. The organiser therefore cannot leave the children unsupervised while the adults prepare another room.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give a total attendance of 21 against the capacity of 24, permit the attendance and require continuing adult supervision. It is wrong if it rejects the event by counting children and adults against separate capacity allowances, counts only the five adults, or says that being below capacity permits the 16 children to remain unsupervised.

**The answer to mark:**

Attendance: 21 people (16 children and 5 adults) is permitted. The Meeting Room maximum is 24, and that figure includes organisers and children (hall-bookings.md, sections 4.2 and 4.4).

Supervision: the children may not be left without adult supervision. All 16 are under 12, and children under 12 must remain under adult supervision (hall-safety.md, section 3.3). At least one adult must therefore stay with them while the others prepare the other room.

No entry in hall-changes.md replaces either section.

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A parent brings their 9-year-old child to a public screening on 16 August 2025 and accompanies them throughout. A volunteer has found an old price list showing that children under 12 pay £2. What is the correct ticket price for the child?

**Expected answer:** The correct ticket price is £3. The £2 price appears in the archived schedule that was superseded on 1 March 2025. The governing schedule charges accompanied children under 12 £3, and no later change alters that concession.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give £3, noting that the child is under 12 and accompanied by an adult. It is wrong if it gives £2 from the archived schedule, £5 for the 12–15 age group, or £7 for standard admission while overlooking the applicable child concession.

The child's ticket is £3, not £2. Children under 12 pay £3 when accompanied by an adult, and the parent is with the child throughout (film-fees.md, section 2.3).

## Answer 9728

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A screening is advertised to begin at 19:30 on 18 November 2025. Someone has paid for a reserved seat but has not arrived when the film starts. Until what exact time must the society hold that seat, and when do the doors normally open?

**Expected answer:** The reserved seat must be held until 19:40, which is 10 minutes after the advertised 19:30 start. Doors normally open at 19:00, which is 30 minutes before the advertised start. The seat-holding deadline is not the same as the door-opening time.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give both 19:40 for the reserved-seat deadline and 19:00 for door opening. It is wrong if it gives 19:30 as the seat deadline, 19:00 as both times, or 20:00 by adding the 30-minute door-opening interval after the start.

**The answer to mark:**

The society must hold the seat until 19:40 on 18 November 2025, which is 10 minutes after the advertised 19:30 start (film-screenings.md, section 3.4). Doors normally open at 19:00, 30 minutes before the advertised start (film-screenings.md, section 3.3).

No entry in film-changes.md replaces either section.

## Answer 9787

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: At a screening on 14 November 2025, a visitor makes a serious threat against another attendee. The visitor has not previously been warned about their behaviour. Must the duty volunteer issue a warning before requiring the visitor to leave?

## Answer 6346

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: Two adults have accepted membership applications. The treasurer receives the first person's £24 payment on 30 June 2025 and the second person's payment on 2 July 2025. How much should each person pay for annual adult membership?

**Expected answer:** The first person pays £24 because the treasurer received payment before 1 July 2025. The second person pays £28 because the revised adult membership fee took effect on 1 July 2025. The payment receipt date, not the application acceptance date, determines which fee applies.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give £24 for 30 June 2025 and £28 for 2 July 2025, explaining that the payment date controls. It is wrong if it charges both £24 using the original fee schedule, both £28 using the latest rate without considering dates, or £20 using the archived schedule.

**The answer to mark:**

The first person pays £24 and the second pays £28.

- **First person:** Their payment was received on 30 June 2025, before 1 July 2025, so the old £24 fee applies (film-changes.md, 1.4).
- **Second person:** Their payment was received on 2 July 2025, after the change took effect, so the new £28 fee applies (film-changes.md, 1.2).

The date the payment is received sets the fee (film-changes.md, 1.3). The dates the applications were accepted don't matter.

- The register says the original documents keep their adopted wording (1.3). That is why section 4.1 still reads 22:00. The register's replacement governs from the effective date (1.1 and 1.4).
- `hall-quick-guide.md` section 5 also says 21:30.

The band's contract says music continues until 21:45. That is 15 minutes past the cutoff.

I found nothing in these documents that lets a contract or a booking override the cutoff. I can't say whether anyone would grant an exception, because that isn't something these documents let me decide.

## Answer 1097

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A member aged 35 has an active annual membership but is suspended by the committee from 1 November through 20 November 2025. They arrive at a public screening on 8 November 2025 and ask to pay the usual member admission price. Are they entitled to the discount, and what admission price applies instead?

**Expected answer:** No, the suspended member is not entitled to the member discount during the suspension. Suspended members cannot claim member prices, even when their annual membership would otherwise be active. The standard admission price on 8 November 2025 is £7 rather than the £4 active-member price.

**Correct requires:** The answer must deny the discount and give £7 as the applicable standard admission charge, contrasting it with the £4 member price. It is wrong if it charges £4 simply because the annual membership has not expired, £5 by confusing the child ticket band or later member rate, or says suspension permanently ends the membership.

**The answer to mark:**

**No, they are not entitled to the member discount. Standard admission of £7 applies.**

- **Suspended members can't claim member prices.** film-membership.md §5.4 says a suspended member cannot claim member prices. The suspension runs 1–20 November 2025, so 8 November falls inside it. This follows film-conduct.md §4.4, which sends membership rights during suspension to film-membership.md.
- **The member price doesn't apply.** The £4 member price (film-fees.md §2.2) is for members with active membership. The change of 19 January 2026 (film-changes.md §3) raises it to £5 only from 1 February 2026, so it doesn't affect an 8 November 2025 screening.
- **The £7 standard price applies.** The 35-year-old doesn't fit any other price category, so film-fees.md §2.1 sets standard admission at £7 per screening. The reduced prices in §2.3 and §2.4 are for children.

## Answer 3628

**Correct requires:** The answer must give £24 for 30 June 2025 and £28 for 2 July 2025, explaining that the payment date controls. It is wrong if it charges both £24 using the original fee schedule, both £28 using the latest rate without considering dates, or £20 using the archived schedule.

**The answer to mark:**

The first person pays £24 and the second pays £28.

The date the payment is received sets the fee. Payments received before 1 July 2025 keep the old £24 fee, and from 1 July 2025 the fee is £28 (film-changes.md, section 1, items 1.2–1.4, which replaces film-fees.md, section 1.1).

- **First person:** payment received 30 June 2025, so £24.
- **Second person:** payment received 2 July 2025, so £28.

## Answer 9515

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: A band is hired for a Saturday evening event on 4 April 2026. Their contract says amplified music will continue until 21:45. The hirer points out that the general hall rules allow Friday and Saturday amplified music until 22:00. May the band play until 21:45?

**Expected answer:** No. From 1 April 2026, amplified music on Friday and Saturday must stop by 21:30. Because the music is being played on 4 April 2026, the revised cutoff applies, and playing until 21:45 would exceed it by 15 minutes.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say no, give 21:30 as the controlling cutoff and identify the proposed 15-minute overrun. It is wrong if it permits 21:45 based on the original 22:00 cutoff, or treats the building's 23:00 Saturday closing time as permission for amplified music until 23:00.

**The answer to mark:**

**No. The band must stop by 21:30, so 21:45 is not allowed.**

- The original rule, `hall-conduct.md` section 4.1, set the Friday and Saturday cutoff at 22:00. That is the rule the hirer is citing.
- Section 5 of `hall-changes.md` replaced it with a 21:30 cutoff, effective 1 April 2026. The decision was made on 20 March 2026.
- The controlling date for that change is the date the music is played. That date is Saturday 4 April 2026, which is after the effective date, so the 21:30 cutoff applies.
- The original `hall-conduct.md` keeps its 22:00 wording, but section 1.3 of the changes register says the replacement entries take priority. The hirer is reading the superseded text.
- The band's contract can't override the cutoff. Nothing in the hall rules lets a hirer or a contract extend the hours. Under section 1.2 of the conduct rules the hirer is responsible for suppliers, so the band is bound by the hall's rule. Playing past 21:30 would be a breach, and section 6 of the conduct rules allows the event to be terminated for serious breaches.

The hirer should shorten the band's set to end by 21:30.

## Answer 6172

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: Four trustees want to attend a committee meeting on 15 November 2026, but one proposes joining by video call from home while the other three attend in person. Do the rules allow a remote trustee to count towards the required quorum, and would that make this meeting quorate?

**Expected answer:** The papers do not say whether trustees may attend remotely or whether remote attendance counts towards the quorum. They require four trustees for a quorum but explicitly leave remote attendance unspecified. Consequently, the papers alone cannot establish whether three trustees attending in person plus one joining by video call would constitute a valid quorum.

**Correct requires:** The answer must identify four as the quorum, state that remote attendance is not addressed and decline to decide whether the proposed meeting is quorate. It is wrong if it automatically counts the video participant and declares a quorum of four, or automatically excludes that participant and declares only three without acknowledging the missing remote-attendance rule.

**The answer to mark:**

The rules don't say whether a remote trustee counts towards the quorum, so they don't settle whether this meeting would be quorate.

- **Quorum:** Section 4.2 of `hall-trustees.md` says "Four trustees form a quorum." It doesn't say whether the four must be physically present.
- **Remote attendance:** Section 6.3 says the rules "do not specify whether trustees may attend remotely." So they neither allow nor forbid a trustee joining by video call, and they say nothing on whether that trustee counts towards the quorum.
- **Changes register:** `hall-changes.md` has no entry that touches sections 4.2 or 6.3, so it changes nothing here.

**Result for 15 November 2026:** Three trustees would be in person and one on video. The meeting reaches four only if the remote trustee counts. The rules don't say either way, so I can't report that the meeting would be quorate. Without the remote trustee, only three are present, which is below the four required by 4.2.

The rules give me no basis to say who decides this. The committee would need to look elsewhere, or take the question to whoever has authority to interpret the rules. The simplest way to avoid doubt is for a fourth trustee to attend in person, which clearly meets 4.2.

## Answer 1230

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: A registered village group has exactly nine local members and supplies that count when booking the Main Hall for two paid hours on 15 October 2025. The organiser requests the village group discount because the group is registered locally. Does it qualify, and what is the room-hire charge without the discount?

**Expected answer:** No, the group does not qualify for the village group discount because the rules require at least ten local members. Being registered is not sufficient when the group has only nine. On 15 October 2025, the Main Hall costs £24 per hour, so two paid hours cost £48 without a discount.

**Correct requires:** The answer must deny the discount because nine is below the ten-member minimum and give £48 as the room charge. It is wrong if it applies the original 20% discount and charges £38.40, applies the archived eight-member qualification and 15% discount to charge £40.80 at the governing hourly rate, or uses the later £28 hourly rate and charges £56.

**The answer to mark:**

No. A registered village group must have at least ten local members, and this group has nine (hall-fees.md, section 3.2). The discount change effective 1 February 2026 does not apply to an event on 15 October 2025, and it only changes the percentage anyway (hall-changes.md, section 3).

Without the discount, the room-hire charge is £48: two paid hours at £24 per hour. The £28 rate takes effect only for events from 1 November 2025, so it does not apply to 15 October 2025 (hall-fees.md, sections 2.1 and 2.4; hall-changes.md, section 2).

## Answer 9817

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: At a screening on 14 November 2025, a visitor makes a serious threat against another attendee. The visitor has not previously been warned about their behaviour. Must the duty volunteer issue a warning before requiring the visitor to leave?

**Expected answer:** No. Serious threats can result in immediate removal without a warning. Although the ordinary disruption procedure allows a volunteer to ask someone to stop and then require them to leave if they refuse, the serious-threat exception permits immediate removal.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say that no prior warning is required for a serious threat and distinguish that exception from the ordinary stop-and-refuse procedure. It is wrong if it insists that the visitor must first receive a warning or refuse an instruction before removal is permitted.

**The answer to mark:**

**No. A warning isn't required.** Section 2.3 of the Fenwray Film Society conduct rules (`reference/film/film-conduct.md`) says "Serious threats may result in immediate removal without a warning." So the duty volunteer can require the visitor to leave straight away, even though they had no earlier warning.

- **Date:** The conduct rules were effective from 1 April 2025, so they applied on 14 November 2025. The committee changes register has no amendment to the conduct rules.
- **Wording:** Section 2.3 says removal "may" follow. It permits immediate removal but doesn't require it. The usual route in 2.1 and 2.2 is to ask the person to stop and then require them to leave if they refuse. Serious threats skip that step.
- **Follow-up:** Under 2.4, the duty volunteer must record the incident within 48 hours. Under 2.5, removal doesn't by itself end the visitor's membership. Under 5.3, it doesn't automatically create a refund right either.

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A parent brings their 9-year-old child to a public screening on 16 August 2025 and accompanies them throughout. A volunteer has found an old price list showing that children under 12 pay £2. What is the correct ticket price for the child?

**Expected answer:** The correct ticket price is £3. The £2 price appears in the archived schedule that was superseded on 1 March 2025. The governing schedule charges accompanied children under 12 £3, and no later change alters that concession.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give £3, noting that the child is under 12 and accompanied by an adult. It is wrong if it gives £2 from the archived schedule, £5 for the 12–15 age group, or £7 for standard admission while overlooking the applicable child concession.

**The answer to mark:**

**The child's ticket costs £3.**

Under film-fees.md section 2.3, children aged under 12 pay £3 when accompanied by an adult. A 9-year-old with a parent throughout meets both conditions.

No changes document replaces section 2.3. The only admission change in film-changes.md is section 3, which covers member admission (2.2) and not children's tickets.

The £2 price is from the old fee schedule, film-fees-archive.md section 2.3. That archive does not govern.

## Answer 8251

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: Four trustees want to attend a committee meeting on 15 November 2026, but one proposes joining by video call from home while the other three attend in person. Do the rules allow a remote trustee to count towards the required quorum, and would that make this meeting quorate?

**Expected answer:** The papers do not say whether trustees may attend remotely or whether remote attendance counts towards the quorum. They require four trustees for a quorum but explicitly leave remote attendance unspecified. Consequently, the papers alone cannot establish whether three trustees attending in person plus one joining by video call would constitute a valid quorum.

**Correct requires:** The answer must identify four as the quorum, state that remote attendance is not addressed and decline to decide whether the proposed meeting is quorate. It is wrong if it automatically counts the video participant and declares a quorum of four, or automatically excludes that participant and declares only three without acknowledging the missing remote-attendance rule.

**The answer to mark:**

The rules don't say, so I can't give a yes or no on either part.

- **Remote trustee counting towards quorum:** The documents don't cover this. Section 6.3 says the rules "do not specify whether trustees may attend remotely" (hall-trustees.md, 6.3). Section 4.2 sets quorum at four trustees but doesn't say whether a remote attendee counts (hall-trustees.md, 4.2). The changes document has nothing on quorum or remote attendance.
- **Whether the meeting is quorate:** This depends on whether the video-call trustee counts as present. If they count, there are four present and the meeting is quorate. If they don't, there are three present and it falls short of the four needed. The rules don't say which applies.

## Answer 1192

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: At a screening on 14 November 2025, a visitor makes a serious threat against another attendee. The visitor has not previously been warned about their behaviour. Must the duty volunteer issue a warning before requiring the visitor to leave?

**Expected answer:** No. Serious threats can result in immediate removal without a warning. Although the ordinary disruption procedure allows a volunteer to ask someone to stop and then require them to leave if they refuse, the serious-threat exception permits immediate removal.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say that no prior warning is required for a serious threat and distinguish that exception from the ordinary stop-and-refuse procedure. It is wrong if it insists that the visitor must first receive a warning or refuse an instruction before removal is permitted.

**The answer to mark:**

**No. The duty volunteer does not have to warn the visitor first.** Serious threats may result in immediate removal without a warning (film-conduct.md, section 2.3).

Section 2.3 is permissive ("may"). It lets the volunteer remove the visitor immediately, but it doesn't require immediate removal. The usual route in section 2.2, where a person who refuses to stop may be required to leave, does not apply as a precondition here.

The changes register (film-changes.md) does not replace anything in film-conduct.md, so section 2.3 applies to the 14 November 2025 screening.

## Answer 2161

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: A band is hired for a Saturday evening event on 4 April 2026. Their contract says amplified music will continue until 21:45. The hirer points out that the general hall rules allow Friday and Saturday amplified music until 22:00. May the band play until 21:45?

**Expected answer:** No. From 1 April 2026, amplified music on Friday and Saturday must stop by 21:30. Because the music is being played on 4 April 2026, the revised cutoff applies, and playing until 21:45 would exceed it by 15 minutes.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say no, give 21:30 as the controlling cutoff and identify the proposed 15-minute overrun. It is wrong if it permits 21:45 based on the original 22:00 cutoff, or treats the building's 23:00 Saturday closing time as permission for amplified music until 23:00.

**The answer to mark:**

No. Amplified music on a Saturday had to stop by 21:30 from 1 April 2026, and the music on 4 April 2026 falls after that date. The band's 21:45 finish is 15 minutes too late (hall-changes.md, section 5, which replaces hall-conduct.md, section 4.1).

The hirer is quoting the old 22:00 cutoff from hall-conduct.md, section 4.1. That wording has been replaced.

## Answer 8776

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: An organiser plans a children's event in the Meeting Room on 19 October 2025. There will be 16 children aged 8 to 11 and five adults, including organisers. The organiser wants to know whether the attendance is permitted and whether the children may be left without adult supervision while the adults prepare another room. What do the rules require?

**Expected answer:** The attendance is permitted because the Meeting Room capacity is 24 people and the total is 21, including the adults and children. However, children under 12 must remain under adult supervision. The organiser therefore cannot leave the children unsupervised while the adults prepare another room.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give a total attendance of 21 against the capacity of 24, permit the attendance and require continuing adult supervision. It is wrong if it rejects the event by counting children and adults against separate capacity allowances, counts only the five adults, or says that being below capacity permits the 16 children to remain unsupervised.

**The answer to mark:**

**Attendance:** It is not permitted. The Meeting Room holds a maximum of 24 people, and capacity includes organisers and children (hall-bookings.md §4.2 and §4.4). 16 children plus 5 adults is 21, which is under 24.

**Supervision:** The children may not be left without adult supervision. Children under 12 must remain under adult supervision (hall-safety.md §3.3). All 16 children are aged 8 to 11, so the rule applies to them. Adults preparing another room does not satisfy it unless some adult stays with the children.

Correction to my attendance line: the attendance **is** permitted, since 21 is within the limit of 24.

No entry in hall-changes.md replaces these sections. The only safety change is to §5.1, which concerns emergency spending.

## Answer 5386

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A screening is advertised to begin at 19:30 on 18 November 2025. Someone has paid for a reserved seat but has not arrived when the film starts. Until what exact time must the society hold that seat, and when do the doors normally open?

**Expected answer:** The reserved seat must be held until 19:40, which is 10 minutes after the advertised 19:30 start. Doors normally open at 19:00, which is 30 minutes before the advertised start. The seat-holding deadline is not the same as the door-opening time.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give both 19:40 for the reserved-seat deadline and 19:00 for door opening. It is wrong if it gives 19:30 as the seat deadline, 19:00 as both times, or 20:00 by adding the 30-minute door-opening interval after the start.

**The answer to mark:**

The society must hold the seat until 19:40 on 18 November 2025. That is 10 minutes after the advertised 19:30 start (film-screenings.md, section 3.4).

Doors normally open at 19:00, which is 30 minutes before the advertised start (film-screenings.md, section 3.3).

The changes register (film-changes.md) doesn't replace either section.

## Answer 3273

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: Two adults have accepted membership applications. The treasurer receives the first person's £24 payment on 30 June 2025 and the second person's payment on 2 July 2025. How much should each person pay for annual adult membership?

**Expected answer:** The first person pays £24 because the treasurer received payment before 1 July 2025. The second person pays £28 because the revised adult membership fee took effect on 1 July 2025. The payment receipt date, not the application acceptance date, determines which fee applies.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give £24 for 30 June 2025 and £28 for 2 July 2025, explaining that the payment date controls. It is wrong if it charges both £24 using the original fee schedule, both £28 using the latest rate without considering dates, or £20 using the archived schedule.

**The answer to mark:**

The first person pays £24 and the second pays £28.

The fee depends on the date the payment is received. Payments received before 1 July 2025 keep the old £24 fee. From 1 July 2025 the fee is £28 (film-changes.md, section 1).

- **First person:** their payment was received on 30 June 2025, so the fee is £24.
- **Second person:** their payment was received on 2 July 2025, so the fee is £28.

## Answer 6631

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: At a screening on 14 November 2025, a visitor makes a serious threat against another attendee. The visitor has not previously been warned about their behaviour. Must the duty volunteer issue a warning before requiring the visitor to leave?

**Expected answer:** No. Serious threats can result in immediate removal without a warning. Although the ordinary disruption procedure allows a volunteer to ask someone to stop and then require them to leave if they refuse, the serious-threat exception permits immediate removal.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say that no prior warning is required for a serious threat and distinguish that exception from the ordinary stop-and-refuse procedure. It is wrong if it insists that the visitor must first receive a warning or refuse an instruction before removal is permitted.

**The answer to mark:**

**No. The duty volunteer does not have to warn the visitor first.** Section 2.3 of `film-conduct.md` says "Serious threats may result in immediate removal without a warning." Threats are prohibited under section 1.2, and the visitor must follow the volunteer's reasonable instructions under section 1.3.

- **Date:** The conduct rules were adopted on 18 March 2025 and took effect on 1 April 2025, so they applied on 14 November 2025. The changes register (`film-changes.md`) has no amendment to the conduct rules.
- **Wording:** Section 2.3 is permissive ("may"), so removal without a warning is allowed but not required. The general route in sections 2.1 and 2.2 is to ask someone to stop and remove them only if they refuse. A serious threat lets the volunteer skip that step.
- **Record:** The volunteer must record the incident within 48 hours (section 2.4).
- **After removal:** Removal doesn't end the visitor's society membership (section 2.5). It doesn't automatically create a refund right either (section 5.3).

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: A hirer books the Meeting Room on 6 September 2025 and asks for one portable speaker and three sets of folding display boards. Ignoring room hire and deposits, what is the total equipment charge?

**Expected answer:** The total equipment charge is £25. The portable speaker costs £10 per booking and folding display boards cost £5 per set per booking. Three sets cost £15, so the speaker and boards together cost £10 + £15 = £25.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give £25 and show £10 + (3 × £5). It is wrong if it charges £15 by treating all three sets of boards as one set, £13 by using the archived speaker and board prices as one-time charges, or £20 by charging for only two sets.

**The answer to mark:**

The total equipment charge is £25.

Equipment is charged per booking: a portable speaker is £10 per booking, and folding display boards are £5 per set per booking. That gives £10 + (3 × £5) = £25 (hall-fees.md, sections 4.2 and 4.3). No changes entry replaces these sections.

## Answer 1442

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: A registered village group has exactly nine local members and supplies that count when booking the Main Hall for two paid hours on 15 October 2025. The organiser requests the village group discount because the group is registered locally. Does it qualify, and what is the room-hire charge without the discount?

**Expected answer:** No, the group does not qualify for the village group discount because the rules require at least ten local members. Being registered is not sufficient when the group has only nine. On 15 October 2025, the Main Hall costs £24 per hour, so two paid hours cost £48 without a discount.

**Correct requires:** The answer must deny the discount because nine is below the ten-member minimum and give £48 as the room charge. It is wrong if it applies the original 20% discount and charges £38.40, applies the archived eight-member qualification and 15% discount to charge £40.80 at the governing hourly rate, or uses the later £28 hourly rate and charges £56.

**The answer to mark:**

The event is on 15 October 2025, before the 1 November 2025 effective date, so the £24 rate applies.

**Discount: it does not qualify.** A registered village group must have at least ten local members (hall-fees.md §3.2). This group has nine, so the 20% discount in §3.1 is unavailable. The changes register doesn't alter this. Its 25% change (hall-changes.md §3) only affects the percentage, and only from 1 February 2026.

**Room-hire charge without the discount: £48.** The Main Hall costs £24 per hour (hall-fees.md §2.1). The Main Hall rate change to £28 takes effect on 1 November 2025 (hall-changes.md §2). The two paid hours meet the two-hour minimum (§2.4). Two hours at £24 is £48.

This is the room-hire charge only. I haven't added any kitchen, equipment or deposit charges.

- **Changes document:** `hall-changes.md` doesn't amend the trustee rules, so nothing there affects this.

**What this means for 15 November 2026:**
- If all four trustees count, the meeting has exactly the four needed and is quorate.
- If the remote trustee can't count, only three are present and the meeting is not quorate. Any decision made then would be at risk, because section 4.3 decisions presuppose a quorate meeting.

**Recommendation:** Don't rely on the remote trustee to make up the numbers. Either:
- have a fourth trustee attend in person, or
- get the committee to agree beforehand, and minute it, that remote attendance counts towards quorum.

The rules don't say who decides this question or how, so a prior committee decision is the safest route.

## Answer 4668

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: Two adults have accepted membership applications. The treasurer receives the first person's £24 payment on 30 June 2025 and the second person's payment on 2 July 2025. How much should each person pay for annual adult membership?

**Expected answer:** The first person pays £24 because the treasurer received payment before 1 July 2025. The second person pays £28 because the revised adult membership fee took effect on 1 July 2025. The payment receipt date, not the application acceptance date, determines which fee applies.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give £24 for 30 June 2025 and £28 for 2 July 2025, explaining that the payment date controls. It is wrong if it charges both £24 using the original fee schedule, both £28 using the latest rate without considering dates, or £20 using the archived schedule.

**The answer to mark:**

**First person: £24. Second person: £28.**

- The fee depends on the date the payment is received. The first person's payment was received on 30 June 2025, before 1 July 2025, so the old fee of £24 applies. The second person's payment was received on 2 July 2025, so the new fee of £28 applies.
- Rule: film-changes.md, section 1 (Decision of 16 June 2025), replaces film-fees.md 1.1. Section 1.2 sets £28 from 1 July 2025. Section 1.3 says the date payment is received determines the fee. Section 1.4 says payments received before 1 July 2025 keep the old fee of £24 (film-fees.md 1.1).

## Answer 7032

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: At a screening on 14 November 2025, a visitor makes a serious threat against another attendee. The visitor has not previously been warned about their behaviour. Must the duty volunteer issue a warning before requiring the visitor to leave?

**Expected answer:** No. Serious threats can result in immediate removal without a warning. Although the ordinary disruption procedure allows a volunteer to ask someone to stop and then require them to leave if they refuse, the serious-threat exception permits immediate removal.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say that no prior warning is required for a serious threat and distinguish that exception from the ordinary stop-and-refuse procedure. It is wrong if it insists that the visitor must first receive a warning or refuse an instruction before removal is permitted.

**The answer to mark:**

**No. The duty volunteer doesn't have to warn the visitor first.**

Section 2.3 of the Conduct and Complaints rules (`film-conduct.md`) says "Serious threats may result in immediate removal without a warning." Section 1.2 prohibits threats. The 14 November 2025 screening falls after the rules took effect on 1 April 2025. The changes register doesn't amend the conduct rules, so section 2.3 applies as written.

- **Discretion:** Section 2.3 says removal "may" happen immediately, so the volunteer can remove the visitor at once without issuing a warning.
- **Ordinary disruption:** For lesser disruption, sections 2.1 and 2.2 apply. The volunteer asks the person to stop, and they can be required to leave only if they refuse. That two-step process doesn't apply to a serious threat.
- **Record:** The volunteer must record the incident within 48 hours (section 2.4).
- **Refund:** Removal doesn't automatically create a refund right (section 5.3). Refund eligibility is decided under `film-refunds.md`.

**For your scenario:**

## Answer 1000

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: An organiser plans a children's event in the Meeting Room on 19 October 2025. There will be 16 children aged 8 to 11 and five adults, including organisers. The organiser wants to know whether the attendance is permitted and whether the children may be left without adult supervision while the adults prepare another room. What do the rules require?

**Expected answer:** The attendance is permitted because the Meeting Room capacity is 24 people and the total is 21, including the adults and children. However, children under 12 must remain under adult supervision. The organiser therefore cannot leave the children unsupervised while the adults prepare another room.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give a total attendance of 21 against the capacity of 24, permit the attendance and require continuing adult supervision. It is wrong if it rejects the event by counting children and adults against separate capacity allowances, counts only the five adults, or says that being below capacity permits the 16 children to remain unsupervised.

**The answer to mark:**

Yes, the attendance is permitted, but the children may not be left without adult supervision.

- **Attendance:** The Meeting Room holds a maximum of 24 people, counting organisers and children, and 16 children plus five adults is 21 (hall-bookings.md, section 4.2 and 4.4).
- **Supervision:** All the children are under 12, so they must remain under adult supervision (hall-safety.md, section 3.3). Preparing another room doesn't create an exception. At least one adult must also stay near the main entrance (hall-safety.md, section 3.4). Some adults can prepare the other room, provided others stay with the children.

- The second person's payment was received on 2 July 2025, so the £28 fee applies.

I didn't check when a payment counts as received for each person. The treasurer received them on 30 June and 2 July. Under film-fees.md section 4.5, a payment is received when cash is handed over or a transfer clears. I took the dates given as the receipt dates under that rule.

## Answer 9147

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A parent brings their 9-year-old child to a public screening on 16 August 2025 and accompanies them throughout. A volunteer has found an old price list showing that children under 12 pay £2. What is the correct ticket price for the child?

**Expected answer:** The correct ticket price is £3. The £2 price appears in the archived schedule that was superseded on 1 March 2025. The governing schedule charges accompanied children under 12 £3, and no later change alters that concession.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give £3, noting that the child is under 12 and accompanied by an adult. It is wrong if it gives £2 from the archived schedule, £5 for the 12–15 age group, or £7 for standard admission while overlooking the applicable child concession.

**The answer to mark:**

The ticket is £3. Children under 12 pay £3 when an adult accompanies them (film-fees.md, section 2.3). The committee changes register doesn't amend that section. The £2 price comes from the superseded schedule in film-fees-archive.md, which doesn't govern.

## Answer 7724

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A screening is advertised to begin at 19:30 on 18 November 2025. Someone has paid for a reserved seat but has not arrived when the film starts. Until what exact time must the society hold that seat, and when do the doors normally open?

**Expected answer:** The reserved seat must be held until 19:40, which is 10 minutes after the advertised 19:30 start. Doors normally open at 19:00, which is 30 minutes before the advertised start. The seat-holding deadline is not the same as the door-opening time.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give both 19:40 for the reserved-seat deadline and 19:00 for door opening. It is wrong if it gives 19:30 as the seat deadline, 19:00 as both times, or 20:00 by adding the 30-minute door-opening interval after the start.

**The answer to mark:**

The society must hold the seat until **19:40**, and doors normally open at **19:00**.

- **Seat held until 19:40.** Reserved seats are held until 10 minutes after the advertised start (film-screenings.md, section 3.4). The advertised start is 19:30.
- **Doors open at 19:00.** Doors open 30 minutes before the advertised start time (film-screenings.md, section 3.3).

The changes register (film-changes.md) doesn't replace either section. It only replaces section 3.1 (maximum audience) of film-screenings.md.
