## Answer 7665

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: A band is hired for a Saturday evening event on 4 April 2026. Their contract says amplified music will continue until 21:45. The hirer points out that the general hall rules allow Friday and Saturday amplified music until 22:00. May the band play until 21:45?

**Expected answer:** No. From 1 April 2026, amplified music on Friday and Saturday must stop by 21:30. Because the music is being played on 4 April 2026, the revised cutoff applies, and playing until 21:45 would exceed it by 15 minutes.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say no, give 21:30 as the controlling cutoff and identify the proposed 15-minute overrun. It is wrong if it permits 21:45 based on the original 22:00 cutoff, or treats the building's 23:00 Saturday closing time as permission for amplified music until 23:00.

**The answer to mark:**

No. Amplified music on a Saturday must stop by 21:30 when it is played on or after 1 April 2026. The 4 April 2026 event is after that date, so the 22:00 cutoff in the general rules no longer applies (hall-changes.md, section 5, replacing hall-conduct.md, section 4.1). The band's 21:45 finish is 15 minutes past the cutoff.

## Answer 6663

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: A registered village group has exactly nine local members and supplies that count when booking the Main Hall for two paid hours on 15 October 2025. The organiser requests the village group discount because the group is registered locally. Does it qualify, and what is the room-hire charge without the discount?

**Expected answer:** No, the group does not qualify for the village group discount because the rules require at least ten local members. Being registered is not sufficient when the group has only nine. On 15 October 2025, the Main Hall costs £24 per hour, so two paid hours cost £48 without a discount.

**Correct requires:** The answer must deny the discount because nine is below the ten-member minimum and give £48 as the room charge. It is wrong if it applies the original 20% discount and charges £38.40, applies the archived eight-member qualification and 15% discount to charge £40.80 at the governing hourly rate, or uses the later £28 hourly rate and charges £56.

**The answer to mark:**

The event is on 15 October 2025, before the 1 November 2025 effective date of the £28 rate. So the original £24 rate applies.

**Qualifies for the discount: No.** hall-fees.md §3.2 requires a registered village group to have at least ten local members. This group has nine. Being registered locally isn't enough on its own. The 25% change in hall-changes.md §3 takes effect on 1 February 2026 and only changes the percentage, so it doesn't apply to this date or alter the ten-member requirement.

**Room-hire charge without the discount: £48.** This is the Main Hall at £24 per hour (hall-fees.md §2.1) for two hours, which is the minimum paid booking (§2.4). The £28 rate in hall-changes.md §2 starts for events on or after 1 November 2025, so it doesn't apply.

## Answer 5101

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: Four trustees want to attend a committee meeting on 15 November 2026, but one proposes joining by video call from home while the other three attend in person. Do the rules allow a remote trustee to count towards the required quorum, and would that make this meeting quorate?

**Expected answer:** The papers do not say whether trustees may attend remotely or whether remote attendance counts towards the quorum. They require four trustees for a quorum but explicitly leave remote attendance unspecified. Consequently, the papers alone cannot establish whether three trustees attending in person plus one joining by video call would constitute a valid quorum.

**Correct requires:** The answer must identify four as the quorum, state that remote attendance is not addressed and decline to decide whether the proposed meeting is quorate. It is wrong if it automatically counts the video participant and declares a quorum of four, or automatically excludes that participant and declares only three without acknowledging the missing remote-attendance rule.

**The answer to mark:**

**The rules don't say, so they don't settle whether the meeting would be quorate.**

- **Quorum:** Rule 4.2 says "Four trustees form a quorum." With three in person and one on video, you would have exactly four trustees, but only if the remote trustee counts.
- **Remote attendance:** Rule 6.3 says the rules "do not specify whether trustees may attend remotely." Nothing elsewhere in the hall rules covers it either. I searched them all for "remote", "video" and "virtual" and found nothing. So nothing allows a remote trustee to count, and nothing forbids it.

**If the remote trustee counts:** the meeting is quorate with exactly four, and 4.3 lets decisions pass by a majority of those present and voting. There would be no margin. If any one of the four dropped out, including through a lost connection, the meeting would fall below quorum.

**If the remote trustee doesn't count:** only three are present, so the meeting is not quorate and can't take valid decisions.

**What I'd suggest:**
1. Have the committee agree, and minute, that remote attendance counts toward quorum for this meeting. This is a gap in the rules, so a committee decision is the natural fix. Ideally they'd do it at a meeting before 15 November. I haven't checked whether the hall's other documents or any outside governing document (a charity constitution, for example) say more, so check that too.
2. Safer still, get a fifth trustee to attend in person, or have the remote trustee attend in person.
3. Longer term, amend the rules to address remote attendance explicitly.

## Answer 8212

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: An organiser plans a children's event in the Meeting Room on 19 October 2025. There will be 16 children aged 8 to 11 and five adults, including organisers. The organiser wants to know whether the attendance is permitted and whether the children may be left without adult supervision while the adults prepare another room. What do the rules require?

**Expected answer:** The attendance is permitted because the Meeting Room capacity is 24 people and the total is 21, including the adults and children. However, children under 12 must remain under adult supervision. The organiser therefore cannot leave the children unsupervised while the adults prepare another room.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give a total attendance of 21 against the capacity of 24, permit the attendance and require continuing adult supervision. It is wrong if it rejects the event by counting children and adults against separate capacity allowances, counts only the five adults, or says that being below capacity permits the 16 children to remain unsupervised.

**The answer to mark:**

Attendance is permitted, but the children may not be left without adult supervision.

- **Attendance:** The Meeting Room holds a maximum of 24 people, counting organisers and children. 16 children plus five adults is 21 (hall-bookings.md, section 4.2 and 4.4).
- **Supervision:** Children under 12 must remain under adult supervision, and all 16 children are 8 to 11. They can't be left alone while the adults prepare another room (hall-safety.md, section 3.3).

If the adults split up, at least one responsible adult must also stay near the main entrance (hall-safety.md, section 3.4). The changes register has no entry that alters any of these sections.

## Answer 8433

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: At a screening on 14 November 2025, a visitor makes a serious threat against another attendee. The visitor has not previously been warned about their behaviour. Must the duty volunteer issue a warning before requiring the visitor to leave?

**Expected answer:** No. Serious threats can result in immediate removal without a warning. Although the ordinary disruption procedure allows a volunteer to ask someone to stop and then require them to leave if they refuse, the serious-threat exception permits immediate removal.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say that no prior warning is required for a serious threat and distinguish that exception from the ordinary stop-and-refuse procedure. It is wrong if it insists that the visitor must first receive a warning or refuse an instruction before removal is permitted.

**The answer to mark:**

**No.** A warning isn't required first. Section 2.3 of the Conduct and Complaints rules (`reference\film\film-conduct.md:22`) says "Serious threats may result in immediate removal without a warning."

- **Rules in force:** The conduct rules were adopted on 18 March 2025 and took effect on 1 April 2025, so they applied on 14 November 2025. The changes register has no amendment to the conduct rules.
- **Threats are prohibited:** Section 1.2 prohibits threats. Section 1.3 requires visitors to follow reasonable instructions from duty volunteers.
- **Removal is discretionary:** Section 2.3 says "may", so the volunteer can remove the visitor immediately but doesn't have to. The usual route in sections 2.1 and 2.2 is to ask the person to stop and require them to leave only if they refuse. A serious threat lets the volunteer skip that step.
- **Record the incident:** The duty volunteer must record it within 48 hours (section 2.4).
- **Other effects:** Removal doesn't end the visitor's society membership (section 2.5), and it doesn't automatically give them a refund right (section 5.3).

**The answer to mark:**

The total equipment charge is **£25**.

- Portable speaker: £10 per booking, so £10 (hall-fees.md §4.2).
- Folding display boards: £5 per set per booking, so 3 × £5 = £15 (hall-fees.md §4.3).

The changes register (hall-changes.md) doesn't replace any equipment charge section. The 6 September 2025 booking is after the 1 April 2025 start date for these charges (hall-fees.md §1.2).

**Expected answer:** The reserved seat must be held until 19:40, which is 10 minutes after the advertised 19:30 start. Doors normally open at 19:00, which is 30 minutes before the advertised start. The seat-holding deadline is not the same as the door-opening time.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give both 19:40 for the reserved-seat deadline and 19:00 for door opening. It is wrong if it gives 19:30 as the seat deadline, 19:00 as both times, or 20:00 by adding the 30-minute door-opening interval after the start.

**The answer to mark:**

The society must hold the seat until 19:40 on 18 November 2025, and doors normally open at 19:00.

- **Seat hold:** Reserved seats are held until 10 minutes after the advertised start, and 19:30 plus 10 minutes is 19:40 (film-screenings.md, section 3.4).
- **Doors:** Doors open 30 minutes before the advertised start time, which is 19:00 (film-screenings.md, section 3.3).

The changes document replaces only section 3.1 of this file, so it doesn't affect sections 3.3 or 3.4.

## Answer 8895

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: An organiser plans a children's event in the Meeting Room on 19 October 2025. There will be 16 children aged 8 to 11 and five adults, including organisers. The organiser wants to know whether the attendance is permitted and whether the children may be left without adult supervision while the adults prepare another room. What do the rules require?

**Expected answer:** The attendance is permitted because the Meeting Room capacity is 24 people and the total is 21, including the adults and children. However, children under 12 must remain under adult supervision. The organiser therefore cannot leave the children unsupervised while the adults prepare another room.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give a total attendance of 21 against the capacity of 24, permit the attendance and require continuing adult supervision. It is wrong if it rejects the event by counting children and adults against separate capacity allowances, counts only the five adults, or says that being below capacity permits the 16 children to remain unsupervised.

**The answer to mark:**

**Attendance: permitted.**
- The Meeting Room holds a maximum of 24 people (hall-bookings.md 4.2).
- Capacity counts organisers and children (4.4).
- 16 children plus 5 adults is 21, which is 3 under the limit.
- The hirer must count attendees when the event begins (hall-safety.md 3.2).
- The fire safety rules in hall-safety.md also apply (hall-bookings.md 4.5).

**Leaving the children unsupervised: not permitted.**
- Children under 12 must remain under adult supervision (hall-safety.md 3.3).
- All the children are aged 8 to 11, so the rule covers every one of them.
- The rule has no exception for adults preparing another room.
- At least one responsible adult must also remain near the main entrance (3.4). That duty is separate from supervising the children.
- The hirer must remain on site throughout the event (hall-conduct.md 1.3).

**What the rules don't say:**
- They set no adult-to-child ratio.
- They don't define "supervision". They don't say whether an adult in a nearby room counts.
- They don't say whether the supervising adult may also be the one at the entrance.

I can't say how those points would be judged. The rules clearly require adult supervision of the children throughout, so the organiser should keep at least one adult with them while the others prepare the other room.

**Other points:**
- 19 October 2025 is a Sunday, so the building closes at 21:00 (hall-bookings.md 3.3) and all guests must leave by then (3.4).
- Setup and clearing must fall within booked hours (3.5).
- The changes register (hall-changes.md) has no entry that affects any of these rules.

## Answer 3756

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: A registered village group has exactly nine local members and supplies that count when booking the Main Hall for two paid hours on 15 October 2025. The organiser requests the village group discount because the group is registered locally. Does it qualify, and what is the room-hire charge without the discount?

**Expected answer:** No, the group does not qualify for the village group discount because the rules require at least ten local members. Being registered is not sufficient when the group has only nine. On 15 October 2025, the Main Hall costs £24 per hour, so two paid hours cost £48 without a discount.

**Correct requires:** The answer must deny the discount because nine is below the ten-member minimum and give £48 as the room charge. It is wrong if it applies the original 20% discount and charges £38.40, applies the archived eight-member qualification and 15% discount to charge £40.80 at the governing hourly rate, or uses the later £28 hourly rate and charges £56.

**The answer to mark:**

**Discount: no.** Hall-fees.md §3.2 says a registered village group must have at least ten local members. This group has nine, so being registered locally isn't enough. Section 3.3 requires the group to give its membership count before booking, and this group did that. The count it gave is below the threshold.

**Room-hire charge without the discount: £48.**
- The Main Hall rate is £24 per hour (hall-fees.md §2.1).
- Two paid hours meets the two-hour minimum (§2.4).
- 2 × £24 = £48.

**Which rules apply:** The event is on 15 October 2025. The committee changes in hall-changes.md don't affect it.
- The Main Hall rise to £28 takes effect for events starting on or after 1 November 2025.
- The change of the discount to 25% takes effect for events starting on or after 1 February 2026.
- The original 20% rule and its ten-member threshold would apply to this date anyway.
- The quick guide's £28 and 25% figures don't apply to this event. The guide is only a summary, and the governing documents prevail over it.

**Not in the £48:** I assumed no kitchen or equipment is hired, since you didn't mention any. The £30 booking deposit (§5.1) counts towards the total hire charge (§5.4).

## Answer 9789

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A member aged 35 has an active annual membership but is suspended by the committee from 1 November through 20 November 2025. They arrive at a public screening on 8 November 2025 and ask to pay the usual member admission price. Are they entitled to the discount, and what admission price applies instead?

**Expected answer:** No, the suspended member is not entitled to the member discount during the suspension. Suspended members cannot claim member prices, even when their annual membership would otherwise be active. The standard admission price on 8 November 2025 is £7 rather than the £4 active-member price.

**Correct requires:** The answer must deny the discount and give £7 as the applicable standard admission charge, contrasting it with the £4 member price. It is wrong if it charges £4 simply because the annual membership has not expired, £5 by confusing the child ticket band or later member rate, or says suspension permanently ends the membership.

**The answer to mark:**

**No, they aren't entitled to the member price. Standard admission of £7 applies.**

- **Suspension removes member prices.** `film-membership.md` 5.4 says "A suspended member cannot vote, book equipment or claim member prices." Conduct rule 4.4 in `film-conduct.md` sends membership rights during suspension to that document. The committee suspended the member from 1 to 20 November 2025, so 8 November falls inside the suspension. The active annual membership doesn't change this.
- **The member rate wouldn't have been £4 anyway.** `film-fees.md` 2.2 sets it at £4, but the 19 January 2026 change in `film-changes.md` (section 3) raised it to £5 from 1 February 2026. That change is tied to the screening date, and 8 November 2025 is before it, so £4 would have applied if they weren't suspended.
- **The price they pay is the standard £7.** `film-fees.md` 2.1 sets standard admission at £7 per screening. The 35-year-old doesn't fit the child rates in 2.3 and 2.4 (under 12, and 12 to 15), and nothing in the documents gives a suspended member a different price.

Suspension doesn't remove their right to attend. Membership rule 4.4 says membership doesn't guarantee admission, and admission still depends on the capacity rules in `film-screenings.md`. I haven't checked those rules here.

## Answer 3868

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: Two adults have accepted membership applications. The treasurer receives the first person's £24 payment on 30 June 2025 and the second person's payment on 2 July 2025. How much should each person pay for annual adult membership?

**Expected answer:** The first person pays £24 because the treasurer received payment before 1 July 2025. The second person pays £28 because the revised adult membership fee took effect on 1 July 2025. The payment receipt date, not the application acceptance date, determines which fee applies.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give £24 for 30 June 2025 and £28 for 2 July 2025, explaining that the payment date controls. It is wrong if it charges both £24 using the original fee schedule, both £28 using the latest rate without considering dates, or £20 using the archived schedule.

**The answer to mark:**

The first person pays £24 and the second pays £28.

- **First person:** The payment was received on 30 June 2025, before 1 July 2025, so the old £24 fee applies (film-changes.md, section 1.4).
- **Second person:** The payment was received on 2 July 2025, so the £28 fee applies from 1 July 2025 (film-changes.md, sections 1.2–1.3). The date the payment is received sets the fee.

## Answer 9239

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: At a screening on 14 November 2025, a visitor makes a serious threat against another attendee. The visitor has not previously been warned about their behaviour. Must the duty volunteer issue a warning before requiring the visitor to leave?

**Expected answer:** No. Serious threats can result in immediate removal without a warning. Although the ordinary disruption procedure allows a volunteer to ask someone to stop and then require them to leave if they refuse, the serious-threat exception permits immediate removal.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say that no prior warning is required for a serious threat and distinguish that exception from the ordinary stop-and-refuse procedure. It is wrong if it insists that the visitor must first receive a warning or refuse an instruction before removal is permitted.

## Answer 4911

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: An organiser plans a children's event in the Meeting Room on 19 October 2025. There will be 16 children aged 8 to 11 and five adults, including organisers. The organiser wants to know whether the attendance is permitted and whether the children may be left without adult supervision while the adults prepare another room. What do the rules require?

**Expected answer:** The attendance is permitted because the Meeting Room capacity is 24 people and the total is 21, including the adults and children. However, children under 12 must remain under adult supervision. The organiser therefore cannot leave the children unsupervised while the adults prepare another room.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give a total attendance of 21 against the capacity of 24, permit the attendance and require continuing adult supervision. It is wrong if it rejects the event by counting children and adults against separate capacity allowances, counts only the five adults, or says that being below capacity permits the 16 children to remain unsupervised.

**The answer to mark:**

**Attendance is permitted, but the children can't be left without adult supervision.**

**Attendance**
- The Meeting Room holds a maximum of 24 people (`hall-bookings.md` §4.2).
- Capacity includes organisers and children (§4.4).
- 16 children plus 5 adults is 21 people, which is within the limit with 3 spare.
- The hirer must count attendees when the event begins (`hall-safety.md` §3.2).

**Supervision**
- Children under 12 must remain under adult supervision (`hall-safety.md` §3.3).
- Children aged 8 to 11 are all under 12, so every child is covered. The rule has no exception for short periods.
- The adults can't all leave to prepare another room. At least one adult must stay supervising the children.
- At least one responsible adult must also remain near the main entrance (§3.4). The rules don't say whether the same adult can cover both duties, so it's safest to keep two adults on duty.
- The named hirer must stay on site throughout the event (`hall-conduct.md` §1.3). The hirer is also responsible for guests (§1.2).
- Preparing another room is fine if it's done by some of the adults, with the others staying with the children.

**Other points**
- 19 October 2025 is a Sunday, so the hall is open 09:00 to 21:00 (`hall-bookings.md` §3.3). Setup and clearing must fall within the booked hours (§3.5).
- None of the entries in `hall-changes.md` affect these rules for that date.

## Answer 1146

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: Four trustees want to attend a committee meeting on 15 November 2026, but one proposes joining by video call from home while the other three attend in person. Do the rules allow a remote trustee to count towards the required quorum, and would that make this meeting quorate?

**Expected answer:** The papers do not say whether trustees may attend remotely or whether remote attendance counts towards the quorum. They require four trustees for a quorum but explicitly leave remote attendance unspecified. Consequently, the papers alone cannot establish whether three trustees attending in person plus one joining by video call would constitute a valid quorum.

**Correct requires:** The answer must identify four as the quorum, state that remote attendance is not addressed and decline to decide whether the proposed meeting is quorate. It is wrong if it automatically counts the video participant and declares a quorum of four, or automatically excludes that participant and declares only three without acknowledging the missing remote-attendance rule.

**The answer to mark:**

The rules don't say. They set quorum at four trustees (hall-trustees.md, section 4.2), but they "do not specify whether trustees may attend remotely" (hall-trustees.md, section 6.3). So I can't tell you whether a remote trustee counts.

It depends on whether the remote trustee counts towards quorum:
- **If the remote trustee counts:** there would be four trustees, which meets the quorum of four.
- **If the remote trustee does not count:** only three would be present, which is below the quorum, so the meeting would not be quorate.

The rules give no basis for choosing between these two.

The hirer points out that the general hall rules allow Friday and Saturday amplified music until 22:00. May the band play until 21:45?

**Expected answer:** No. From 1 April 2026, amplified music on Friday and Saturday must stop by 21:30. Because the music is being played on 4 April 2026, the revised cutoff applies, and playing until 21:45 would exceed it by 15 minutes.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say no, give 21:30 as the controlling cutoff and identify the proposed 15-minute overrun. It is wrong if it permits 21:45 based on the original 22:00 cutoff, or treats the building's 23:00 Saturday closing time as permission for amplified music until 23:00.

**The answer to mark:**

**No. The band may not play until 21:45. Amplified music must stop by 21:30.**

4 April 2026 is a Saturday, and the music is played on that date. That is after the 1 April 2026 effective date, so the changes register governs. Hall-changes.md section 5 (5.1–5.3) replaces hall-conduct.md section 4.1. It says Friday and Saturday amplified music must stop by 21:30. The 22:00 cutoff in the original 4.1 no longer applies.

## Answer 2843

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: Four trustees want to attend a committee meeting on 15 November 2026, but one proposes joining by video call from home while the other three attend in person. **Expected answer:** The papers do not say whether trustees may attend remotely or whether remote attendance counts towards the quorum. They require four trustees for a quorum but explicitly leave remote attendance unspecified. Consequently, the papers alone cannot establish whether three trustees attending in person plus one joining by video call would constitute a valid quorum.

It is wrong if it automatically counts the video participant and declares a quorum of four, or automatically excludes that participant and declares only three without acknowledging the missing remote-attendance rule.

**The answer to mark:**

The documents don't say, so I can't give a yes or no on either part.

- **Remote trustee counting towards quorum:** The rules say they "do not specify whether trustees may attend remotely" (hall-trustees.md, section 6.3). They don't allow or bar a video-call trustee from counting.
- **Whether the meeting is quorate:** Quorum is four trustees (hall-trustees.md, section 4.2). The three in person are short of four. The meeting is quorate only if the remote trustee counts as present, and the documents don't settle that. No changes entry replaces section 4.2 or 6.3 (hall-changes.md).

## Answer 3385

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A member aged 35 has an active annual membership but is suspended by the committee from 1 November through 20 November 2025. They arrive at a public screening on 8 November 2025 and ask to pay the usual member admission price. Are they entitled to the discount, and what admission price applies instead?

**Expected answer:** No, the suspended member is not entitled to the member discount during the suspension. Suspended members cannot claim member prices, even when their annual membership would otherwise be active. The standard admission price on 8 November 2025 is £7 rather than the £4 active-member price.

**Correct requires:** The answer must deny the discount and give £7 as the applicable standard admission charge, contrasting it with the £4 member price. It is wrong if it charges £4 simply because the annual membership has not expired, £5 by confusing the child ticket band or later member rate, or says suspension permanently ends the membership.

**The answer to mark:**

No. A suspended member cannot claim member prices (film-membership.md, 5.4), so the suspension from 1 to 20 November 2025 covers 8 November 2025.

The standard admission price of £7 applies instead (film-fees.md, 2.1). The 1 February 2026 change to the member price doesn't affect this.

## Answer 4890

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: Two adults have accepted membership applications. The treasurer receives the first person's £24 payment on 30 June 2025 and the second person's payment on 2 July 2025. How much should each person pay for annual adult membership?

**Expected answer:** The first person pays £24 because the treasurer received payment before 1 July 2025. The second person pays £28 because the revised adult membership fee took effect on 1 July 2025. The payment receipt date, not the application acceptance date, determines which fee applies.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give £24 for 30 June 2025 and £28 for 2 July 2025, explaining that the payment date controls. It is wrong if it charges both £24 using the original fee schedule, both £28 using the latest rate without considering dates, or £20 using the archived schedule.

**The answer to mark:**

**The first person pays £24 and the second pays £28.**

- Under `film-fees.md` §1.1, annual adult membership was £24.
- The committee decision of 16 June 2025 (`film-changes.md` §1) raised it to £28 from 1 July 2025. Under §1.3–1.4, the date the payment is received sets the fee, and payments received before 1 July 2025 keep the old fee.
- `film-membership.md` §2.5 says payment is due only after the application is accepted. The application dates therefore don't matter.
- The first payment was received on 30 June 2025, before the change, so the fee is £24.
- The second payment was received on 2 July 2025, after the change, so the fee is £28.

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: At a screening on 14 November 2025, a visitor makes a serious threat against another attendee. The visitor has not previously been warned about their behaviour. Must the duty volunteer issue a warning before requiring the visitor to leave?

**The answer to mark:**

No. A warning isn't required. Serious threats may result in immediate removal without a warning (film-conduct.md, section 2.3). The warning-first process applies to disruptive behaviour: a person who refuses to stop may then be required to leave (section 2.2).

The changes register has no entry that replaces section 2.3 or 2.2 of the conduct document, so both apply to the 14 November 2025 date.

## Answer 5760

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: An organiser plans a children's event in the Meeting Room on 19 October 2025. There will be 16 children aged 8 to 11 and five adults, including organisers. The organiser wants to know whether the attendance is permitted and whether the children may be left without adult supervision while the adults prepare another room. What do the rules require?

**Expected answer:** The attendance is permitted because the Meeting Room capacity is 24 people and the total is 21, including the adults and children. However, children under 12 must remain under adult supervision. The organiser therefore cannot leave the children unsupervised while the adults prepare another room.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give a total attendance of 21 against the capacity of 24, permit the attendance and require continuing adult supervision. It is wrong if it rejects the event by counting children and adults against separate capacity allowances, counts only the five adults, or says that being below capacity permits the 16 children to remain unsupervised.

**The answer to mark:**

**Attendance: not permitted as planned.**

- The Meeting Room holds a maximum of 24 people (hall-bookings.md 4.2).
- Capacity includes organisers, performers and children (4.4).
- 16 children plus 5 adults is 21 people, which is under the limit.

So attendance is permitted. The 24 limit is the only attendance cap I found for the Meeting Room, and nothing in the changes register alters it.

**Supervision: the children may not be left unsupervised.**

- Children under 12 must remain under adult supervision (hall-safety.md 3.3). All the children are aged 8 to 11, so this applies to every one of them.
- The rules don't say how many adults must supervise or what counts as supervision. They only say the children must remain under it. Leaving the 16 children alone while all the adults prepare another room would breach 3.3.
- At least one responsible adult must also stay near the main entrance (3.4). That duty is separate from supervising the children.
- Some of the five adults could prepare the other room, as long as enough adults stay with the children and one adult remains near the main entrance. The same adult could cover both duties only if that adult can supervise the children from the entrance. The rules don't settle that point.

**Other points that apply**

- The named hirer must be an adult aged 18 or over, and must stay on site throughout the event (hall-bookings.md 1.2; hall-conduct.md 1.3).
- The hirer must count attendees when the event begins (hall-safety.md 3.2).
- The hirer must explain the emergency exits to organisers (hall-safety.md 1.3).
- 19 October 2025 is a Sunday, so hall use must finish by 21:00 (hall-bookings.md 3.3). The Monday-to-Thursday hours change doesn't affect Sundays.
- Using the second room adds to the building total, which can't exceed 100 people (hall-bookings.md 4.3).

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A screening is advertised to begin at 19:30 on 18 November 2025. Someone has paid for a reserved seat but has not arrived when the film starts. Until what exact time must the society hold that seat, and when do the doors normally open?

**Correct requires:** The answer must give both 19:40 for the reserved-seat deadline and 19:00 for door opening. It is wrong if it gives 19:30 as the seat deadline, 19:00 as both times, or 20:00 by adding the 30-minute door-opening interval after the start.

Under the Fenwray Film Society's screening rules (`reference\film\film-screenings.md`):

- **Seat hold:** The society must hold the reserved seat until **19:40** on 18 November 2025. Section 3.4 says reserved seats are held until 10 minutes after the advertised start.
- **Doors:** Doors normally open at **19:00**, 30 minutes before the advertised start (section 3.3).

The committee changes register (`film-changes.md`) has no amendment that affects either rule. Its only change to this file is to the maximum audience in 3.1.

A late arrival can enter only during a suitable break (section 3.5). Holding the seat until 19:40 doesn't guarantee entry at that time.

The 22:00 cutoff in hall-conduct.md section 4.1 was replaced by hall-changes.md section 5 (5.1–5.2). That section sets the Friday and Saturday cutoff at 21:30, effective 1 April 2026. The controlling date is the date the music is played (section 5.3). That date is 4 April 2026, which is after the effective date.

## Answer 8564

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A screening is advertised to begin at 19:30 on 18 November 2025. Someone has paid for a reserved seat but has not arrived when the film starts. Until what exact time must the society hold that seat, and when do the doors normally open?

**Expected answer:** The reserved seat must be held until 19:40, which is 10 minutes after the advertised 19:30 start. Doors normally open at 19:00, which is 30 minutes before the advertised start. The seat-holding deadline is not the same as the door-opening time.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give both 19:40 for the reserved-seat deadline and 19:00 for door opening. It is wrong if it gives 19:30 as the seat deadline, 19:00 as both times, or 20:00 by adding the 30-minute door-opening interval after the start.

**The answer to mark:**

The society must hold the seat until **19:40** on 18 November 2025. Doors normally open at **19:00**.

- **Seat hold:** Reserved seats are held until 10 minutes after the advertised start (film-screenings.md, section 3.4). The advertised start is 19:30, so the hold ends at 19:40.
- **Doors:** Doors open 30 minutes before the advertised start (film-screenings.md, section 3.3). That is 19:00.

No entry in film-changes.md replaces sections 3.3 or 3.4. The only change to film-screenings.md is to section 3.1, the maximum audience.

**Leaving the children unsupervised:** Not permitted. All the children are aged 8 to 11, and children under 12 must remain under adult supervision (hall-safety.md §3.3). At least one responsible adult must also stay near the main entrance (hall-safety.md §3.4).

## Answer 6199

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: A registered village group has exactly nine local members and supplies that count when booking the Main Hall for two paid hours on 15 October 2025. The organiser requests the village group discount because the group is registered locally. Does it qualify, and what is the room-hire charge without the discount?

**Expected answer:** No, the group does not qualify for the village group discount because the rules require at least ten local members. Being registered is not sufficient when the group has only nine. On 15 October 2025, the Main Hall costs £24 per hour, so two paid hours cost £48 without a discount.

**Correct requires:** The answer must deny the discount because nine is below the ten-member minimum and give £48 as the room charge. It is wrong if it applies the original 20% discount and charges £38.40, applies the archived eight-member qualification and 15% discount to charge £40.80 at the governing hourly rate, or uses the later £28 hourly rate and charges £56.

**The answer to mark:**

For an event on 15 October 2025, the group does not qualify, and the room-hire charge without the discount is £48.

**Eligibility.** hall-fees.md 3.2 says a registered village group must have at least ten local members. This group has nine, so it falls short. Being registered locally isn't enough. Under 3.3 the group must provide its membership count before booking, and it has supplied nine.

**Which rules apply.** The changes register has two entries that could matter here, and neither alters the result:
- The 25% discount (changes 3.1–3.3) takes effect on 1 February 2026. The event date of 15 October 2025 is earlier, so the original 20% wording of 3.1 stays in force. The ten-member minimum in 3.2 isn't replaced in either case.
- The £28 hourly rate (changes 2.1–2.3) takes effect on 1 November 2025. The event date is earlier, so the original rate of £24 per hour applies (hall-fees.md 2.1).

**Charge.** Two hours at £24 is **£48**. Two hours also meets the two-hour minimum in 2.4. Any kitchen, equipment or deposit charges would be added separately and aren't included in this figure.

## Answer 7366

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: A band is hired for a Saturday evening event on 4 April 2026. Their contract says amplified music will continue until 21:45. The hirer points out that the general hall rules allow Friday and Saturday amplified music until 22:00. May the band play until 21:45?

**Expected answer:** No. From 1 April 2026, amplified music on Friday and Saturday must stop by 21:30. Because the music is being played on 4 April 2026, the revised cutoff applies, and playing until 21:45 would exceed it by 15 minutes.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say no, give 21:30 as the controlling cutoff and identify the proposed 15-minute overrun. It is wrong if it permits 21:45 based on the original 22:00 cutoff, or treats the building's 23:00 Saturday closing time as permission for amplified music until 23:00.

**The answer to mark:**

No. Saturday amplified music must stop by 21:30 for music played on or after 1 April 2026, which includes 4 April 2026. This replaced the 22:00 cutoff (hall-changes.md, section 5; hall-conduct.md, section 4.1). The band's 21:45 finish is 15 minutes past that limit.

- **Whether the meeting is quorate:** Quorum is four trustees (hall-trustees.md, section 4.2). If the remote trustee counts, four are present and the meeting is quorate. If they don't count, only three are present and it isn't. The rules don't say which applies.

I found nothing in hall-changes.md that touches quorum or remote attendance.

## Answer 5417

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: At a screening on 14 November 2025, a visitor makes a serious threat against another attendee. The visitor has not previously been warned about their behaviour. Must the duty volunteer issue a warning before requiring the visitor to leave?

**Expected answer:** No. Serious threats can result in immediate removal without a warning. Although the ordinary disruption procedure allows a volunteer to ask someone to stop and then require them to leave if they refuse, the serious-threat exception permits immediate removal.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say that no prior warning is required for a serious threat and distinguish that exception from the ordinary stop-and-refuse procedure. It is wrong if it insists that the visitor must first receive a warning or refuse an instruction before removal is permitted.

**The answer to mark:**

**No.** The duty volunteer does not have to warn the visitor first. For a serious threat, the visitor may be removed immediately.

- film-conduct.md §2.3: "Serious threats may result in immediate removal without a warning."
- Threats are prohibited under §1.2.
- Visitors must follow reasonable instructions from duty volunteers under §1.3.

The warning-first route in §2.1–2.2 applies to disruptive behaviour. There, a person who refuses to stop may be required to leave.

film-changes.md has no amendment to these conduct sections, so the 14 November 2025 date doesn't alter the answer.

The £4 member rate in film-fees.md 2.2 is for members with active membership. Their membership is still active, but 5.4 overrides it during the suspension.

**Leaving the children unsupervised: not permitted.**

## Answer 3806

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: Four trustees want to attend a committee meeting on 15 November 2026, but one proposes joining by video call from home while the other three attend in person. Do the rules allow a remote trustee to count towards the required quorum, and would that make this meeting quorate?

**Expected answer:** The papers do not say whether trustees may attend remotely or whether remote attendance counts towards the quorum. They require four trustees for a quorum but explicitly leave remote attendance unspecified. Consequently, the papers alone cannot establish whether three trustees attending in person plus one joining by video call would constitute a valid quorum.

**Correct requires:** The answer must identify four as the quorum, state that remote attendance is not addressed and decline to decide whether the proposed meeting is quorate. It is wrong if it automatically counts the video participant and declares a quorum of four, or automatically excludes that participant and declares only three without acknowledging the missing remote-attendance rule.

**The answer to mark:**

**The rules don't say whether a remote trustee counts towards quorum, so I can't give a definite yes or no. Whether the meeting is quorate depends on a question the rules leave open.**

- **Quorum:** Rule 4.2 says "Four trustees form a quorum." The rules don't say those four must be physically present.
- **Remote attendance:** Rule 6.3 says the rules "do not specify whether trustees may attend remotely." Nothing else in the hall documents mentions remote or video attendance either.
- **Your meeting:** Three trustees in the room plus one on video makes four attending. The meeting is quorate only if the video attendee counts as one of the four.

**If the remote trustee counts:** the meeting is quorate with exactly four. Any one of the four dropping out, for example through a connection failure, would make it inquorate. Decisions need a majority of those present and voting (4.3), and the chair has a casting vote on a tie (4.4).

**If the remote trustee doesn't count:** only three are present, so the meeting is inquorate and can't make valid decisions.

Because the rules are silent, the safer course is one of these:
1. Have the committee agree, ideally minuted at an earlier meeting, that remote attendees count towards quorum.
2. Get a fourth trustee to attend in person on 15 November 2026. The rules give the committee five to nine trustees (2.1), so at least one more should be available.
3. Check whether the hall has any other governing document, such as a constitution, that covers remote attendance. I found nothing in the files here.

If the meeting goes ahead on the video-call basis alone, any decisions could be challenged as made without a quorum.

## Answer 1985

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A member aged 35 has an active annual membership but is suspended by the committee from 1 November through 20 November 2025. They arrive at a public screening on 8 November 2025 and ask to pay the usual member admission price. Are they entitled to the discount, and what admission price applies instead?

**Expected answer:** No, the suspended member is not entitled to the member discount during the suspension. Suspended members cannot claim member prices, even when their annual membership would otherwise be active. The standard admission price on 8 November 2025 is £7 rather than the £4 active-member price.

**Correct requires:** The answer must deny the discount and give £7 as the applicable standard admission charge, contrasting it with the £4 member price. The suspension runs 1–20 November 2025, so 8 November falls inside it. That makes it 20 days, within the 30-day maximum in `film-conduct.md` §4.1. The membership stays active but the member price can't be claimed. (`film-conduct.md` §4.4 sends membership rights during suspension to `film-membership.md`.)
- **The discount needs an active membership.** `film-fees.md` §2.2 gives the member price only to "members with active membership." The suspension rule in §5.4 overrides that for the suspended period.

- **The later £5 member price is irrelevant.** The 19 January 2026 decision in `film-changes.md` (§3) raises the member price to £5, but only from 1 February 2026. A screening on 8 November 2025 isn't affected, and it wouldn't change the answer anyway.

The member can ask for a review within seven calendar days of the suspension decision (`film-conduct.md` §4.5). Nothing in these documents lets a volunteer waive the rule at the door. The quick guide (§5.2) says not to promise an exception without committee authority.

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: A hirer books the Meeting Room on 6 September 2025 and asks for one portable speaker and three sets of folding display boards. Ignoring room hire and deposits, what is the total equipment charge?

**Expected answer:** The total equipment charge is £25. The portable speaker costs £10 per booking and folding display boards cost £5 per set per booking. Three sets cost £15, so the speaker and boards together cost £10 + £15 = £25.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give £25 and show £10 + (3 × £5). It is wrong if it charges £15 by treating all three sets of boards as one set, £13 by using the archived speaker and board prices as one-time charges, or £20 by charging for only two sets.

**The answer to mark:**

The total equipment charge is **£25**.

- Portable speaker: £10 per booking, so £10 (hall-fees.md §4.2).
- Folding display boards: £5 per set per booking, so 3 × £5 = £15 (hall-fees.md §4.3).

No changes entry replaces the equipment charges in §4. The charges apply because the booking starts after 1 April 2025 (hall-fees.md §1.2).

## Answer 4392

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: At a screening on 14 November 2025, a visitor makes a serious threat against another attendee. The visitor has not previously been warned about their behaviour. Must the duty volunteer issue a warning before requiring the visitor to leave?

**Expected answer:** No. Serious threats can result in immediate removal without a warning. Although the ordinary disruption procedure allows a volunteer to ask someone to stop and then require them to leave if they refuse, the serious-threat exception permits immediate removal.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say that no prior warning is required for a serious threat and distinguish that exception from the ordinary stop-and-refuse procedure. It is wrong if it insists that the visitor must first receive a warning or refuse an instruction before removal is permitted.

**The answer to mark:**

No. Serious threats may result in immediate removal without a warning, so the duty volunteer doesn't have to warn the visitor first (film-conduct.md, section 2.3). The volunteer must record the incident within 48 hours (section 2.4).

## Answer 6829

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A screening is advertised to begin at 19:30 on 18 November 2025. Someone has paid for a reserved seat but has not arrived when the film starts. Until what exact time must the society hold that seat, and when do the doors normally open?

**Expected answer:** The reserved seat must be held until 19:40, which is 10 minutes after the advertised 19:30 start. Doors normally open at 19:00, which is 30 minutes before the advertised start. The seat-holding deadline is not the same as the door-opening time.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give both 19:40 for the reserved-seat deadline and 19:00 for door opening. It is wrong if it gives 19:30 as the seat deadline, 19:00 as both times, or 20:00 by adding the 30-minute door-opening interval after the start.

**The answer to mark:**

The society must hold the seat until **19:40** on 18 November 2025. That is 10 minutes after the advertised 19:30 start (film-screenings.md §3.4). Doors normally open at **19:00**, 30 minutes before the advertised start (§3.3).

None of the committee changes affect these timings. The only change to film-screenings.md is the audience cap, which applies from 1 June 2026 and doesn't touch §3.3 or §3.4.

## Answer 1546

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: At a screening on 14 November 2025, a visitor makes a serious threat against another attendee. The visitor has not previously been warned about their behaviour. Must the duty volunteer issue a warning before requiring the visitor to leave?

**Expected answer:** No. Serious threats can result in immediate removal without a warning. Although the ordinary disruption procedure allows a volunteer to ask someone to stop and then require them to leave if they refuse, the serious-threat exception permits immediate removal.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say that no prior warning is required for a serious threat and distinguish that exception from the ordinary stop-and-refuse procedure. It is wrong if it insists that the visitor must first receive a warning or refuse an instruction before removal is permitted.

**The answer to mark:**

**No. A warning isn't required first.**

Under film-conduct.md, section 2.3, "Serious threats may result in immediate removal without a warning." Section 1.2 prohibits threats, and section 1.3 requires visitors to follow reasonable instructions from duty volunteers.

- **Warning:** The ordinary route in sections 2.1 and 2.2 is to ask someone to stop, then require them to leave if they refuse. Section 2.3 lets a duty volunteer skip that step for a serious threat. The visitor's lack of a previous warning doesn't change this.
- **Discretion:** The rule says "may", so immediate removal is permitted rather than compelled. The document doesn't define "serious threat". Whether this threat counts as serious is a judgment the document leaves open.
- **Record:** The duty volunteer must record the incident within 48 hours (2.4).
- **Refund:** Removal doesn't automatically give the visitor a refund right (5.3). Eligibility is decided under film-refunds.md (5.4).

The film-changes.md amendments don't touch the conduct rules, so the 14 November 2025 date doesn't alter this answer. The conduct rules took effect on 1 April 2025, so they applied on that date.

## Answer 7353

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A parent brings their 9-year-old child to a public screening on 16 August 2025 and accompanies them throughout. A volunteer has found an old price list showing that children under 12 pay £2. What is the correct ticket price for the child?

**Expected answer:** The correct ticket price is £3. The £2 price appears in the archived schedule that was superseded on 1 March 2025. The governing schedule charges accompanied children under 12 £3, and no later change alters that concession.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give £3, noting that the child is under 12 and accompanied by an adult. It is wrong if it gives £2 from the archived schedule, £5 for the 12–15 age group, or £7 for standard admission while overlooking the applicable child concession.

**The answer to mark:**

The child's ticket is £3. A child under 12 pays £3 when accompanied by an adult (film-fees.md, section 2.3). The child is 9 and the parent is with them throughout, so this applies.

No entry in film-changes.md replaces section 2.3, so it governs for a screening on 16 August 2025. The £2 price list isn't part of the governing rules.

## Answer 3270

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A member aged 35 has an active annual membership but is suspended by the committee from 1 November through 20 November 2025. They arrive at a public screening on 8 November 2025 and ask to pay the usual member admission price. Are they entitled to the discount, and what admission price applies instead?

**Expected answer:** No, the suspended member is not entitled to the member discount during the suspension. Suspended members cannot claim member prices, even when their annual membership would otherwise be active. The standard admission price on 8 November 2025 is £7 rather than the £4 active-member price.

**Correct requires:** The answer must deny the discount and give £7 as the applicable standard admission charge, contrasting it with the £4 member price. It is wrong if it charges £4 simply because the annual membership has not expired, £5 by confusing the child ticket band or later member rate, or says suspension permanently ends the membership.

**The answer to mark:**

**No, they aren't entitled to the member discount. They pay the standard admission price of £7.**

- **Suspension:** Under `film-membership.md` 5.4, a suspended member "cannot … claim member prices." The suspension runs 1–20 November 2025 and is 20 days long, within the 30-day maximum in `film-conduct.md` 4.1. The screening on 8 November falls inside it, so having an active annual membership doesn't help.
- **Price:** The member rate in `film-fees.md` 2.2 only applies to "members with active membership." This person is aged 35, so none of the child rates (2.3, 2.4) apply. That leaves standard admission at £7 per screening (`film-fees.md` 2.1). The changes register doesn't amend that figure.
- **Member rate on that date:** The member rate on 8 November 2025 would have been £4. The increase to £5 only applies to screenings from 1 February 2026 (`film-changes.md` 3.2–3.3). It doesn't matter here because the discount isn't available.

Once the suspension ends after 20 November, they can claim the member price again for later screenings.

## Answer 9556

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: A band is hired for a Saturday evening event on 4 April 2026. Their contract says amplified music will continue until 21:45. The hirer points out that the general hall rules allow Friday and Saturday amplified music until 22:00. May the band play until 21:45?

**Expected answer:** No. From 1 April 2026, amplified music on Friday and Saturday must stop by 21:30. Because the music is being played on 4 April 2026, the revised cutoff applies, and playing until 21:45 would exceed it by 15 minutes.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say no, give 21:30 as the controlling cutoff and identify the proposed 15-minute overrun. It is wrong if it permits 21:45 based on the original 22:00 cutoff, or treats the building's 23:00 Saturday closing time as permission for amplified music until 23:00.

**The answer to mark:**

**No. The band may not play until 21:45. Amplified music must stop by 21:30.**

The hirer is relying on hall-conduct.md section 4.1, which sets 22:00 on Friday and Saturday. The changes register replaces that section. Under hall-changes.md section 5 (decision of 20 March 2026), from 1 April 2026 section 4.1 is replaced by "Friday and Saturday amplified music must stop by 21:30" (5.1, 5.2).

The controlling date is the date the music is played (5.3). That is 4 April 2026, after the 1 April effective date, so the 21:30 limit applies.

## Answer 6534

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: Two adults have accepted membership applications. The treasurer receives the first person's £24 payment on 30 June 2025 and the second person's payment on 2 July 2025. How much should each person pay for annual adult membership?

**Expected answer:** The first person pays £24 because the treasurer received payment before 1 July 2025. The second person pays £28 because the revised adult membership fee took effect on 1 July 2025. The payment receipt date, not the application acceptance date, determines which fee applies.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give £24 for 30 June 2025 and £28 for 2 July 2025, explaining that the payment date controls. It is wrong if it charges both £24 using the original fee schedule, both £28 using the latest rate without considering dates, or £20 using the archived schedule.

**The answer to mark:**

The first person pays £24 and the second pays £28.

- **First person (payment received 30 June 2025):** £24. The committee decision of 16 June 2025 raised the adult fee to £28 from 1 July 2025. Under section 1.3, the date the payment is received sets the fee. Under section 1.4, payments received before 1 July 2025 keep the old fee. Section 1.1 of film-fees.md sets that old fee at £24. (film-changes.md, section 1)
- **Second person (payment received 2 July 2025):** £28. The same decision applies, because the payment was received on or after 1 July 2025. (film-changes.md, sections 1.2–1.3)

## Answer 2227

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: At a screening on 14 November 2025, a visitor makes a serious threat against another attendee. The visitor has not previously been warned about their behaviour. Must the duty volunteer issue a warning before requiring the visitor to leave?

**Expected answer:** No. Serious threats can result in immediate removal without a warning. Although the ordinary disruption procedure allows a volunteer to ask someone to stop and then require them to leave if they refuse, the serious-threat exception permits immediate removal.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say that no prior warning is required for a serious threat and distinguish that exception from the ordinary stop-and-refuse procedure. It is wrong if it insists that the visitor must first receive a warning or refuse an instruction before removal is permitted.

**The answer to mark:**

**No.** Under section 2.3 of the conduct rules (`reference/film/film-conduct.md`), "Serious threats may result in immediate removal without a warning." The visitor's lack of a previous warning doesn't change that.

- **Threats are prohibited.** Section 1.2 bans threats, and section 1.3 requires visitors to follow reasonable instructions from duty volunteers.
- **The warning step is for ordinary disruption.** Sections 2.1 and 2.2 let a volunteer ask someone to stop disruptive behaviour, and a person who refuses may then be required to leave. A serious threat skips that step.
- **Removal is discretionary.** Section 2.3 says "may," so the volunteer can choose to warn first. They aren't required to.
- **The volunteer must record the incident within 48 hours** (section 2.4). For a 14 November 2025 incident, that deadline falls on 16 November 2025.
- **Other consequences:**
  - Removal doesn't end membership (section 2.5).
  - It doesn't automatically create a refund right (sections 5.3 and 5.4).

The conduct rules took effect on 1 April 2025. The changes register (`film-changes.md`) doesn't amend them, so the rules above applied on 14 November 2025.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give both 19:40 for the reserved-seat deadline and 19:00 for door opening. It is wrong if it gives 19:30 as the seat deadline, 19:00 as both times, or 20:00 by adding the 30-minute door-opening interval after the start.

**The answer to mark:**

The society must hold the seat until **19:40** on 18 November 2025, and doors normally open at **19:00**.

- **Seat hold:** Reserved seats are held until 10 minutes after the advertised start (film-screenings.md, section 3.4). The advertised start is 19:30, so the hold runs to 19:40.
- **Doors:** Doors open 30 minutes before the advertised start time (film-screenings.md, section 3.3). That puts the opening at 19:00.

The changes register (film-changes.md) replaces only section 3.1 of film-screenings.md, which covers maximum audience. It doesn't affect sections 3.3 or 3.4.

## Answer 6435

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: An organiser plans a children's event in the Meeting Room on 19 October 2025. There will be 16 children aged 8 to 11 and five adults, including organisers. The organiser wants to know whether the attendance is permitted and whether the children may be left without adult supervision while the adults prepare another room. What do the rules require?

**Expected answer:** The attendance is permitted because the Meeting Room capacity is 24 people and the total is 21, including the adults and children. However, children under 12 must remain under adult supervision. The organiser therefore cannot leave the children unsupervised while the adults prepare another room.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give a total attendance of 21 against the capacity of 24, permit the attendance and require continuing adult supervision. It is wrong if it rejects the event by counting children and adults against separate capacity allowances, counts only the five adults, or says that being below capacity permits the 16 children to remain unsupervised.

**The answer to mark:**

**Attendance: permitted.** There will be 16 children and 5 adults, so 21 people in the Meeting Room. Hall Bookings §4.2 sets the Meeting Room maximum at 24, and §4.4 says that count includes organisers and children. No changes document replaces §4.

**Leaving the children unsupervised: not permitted.** Hall Safety §3.3 says children under 12 must remain under adult supervision. All the children are aged 8 to 11, so the rule covers every one of them. The rules give no exception for adults preparing another room. Hall Safety §3.4 also requires at least one responsible adult to stay near the main entrance. The changes register (hall-changes.md) does not replace §3.3 or §3.4.

The rules don't define "adult supervision", for example whether an adult must be in the same room. I have not assumed a meaning.

## Answer 7683

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: Four trustees want to attend a committee meeting on 15 November 2026, but one proposes joining by video call from home while the other three attend in person. Do the rules allow a remote trustee to count towards the required quorum, and would that make this meeting quorate?

**Expected answer:** The papers do not say whether trustees may attend remotely or whether remote attendance counts towards the quorum. They require four trustees for a quorum but explicitly leave remote attendance unspecified. Consequently, the papers alone cannot establish whether three trustees attending in person plus one joining by video call would constitute a valid quorum.

**Correct requires:** The answer must identify four as the quorum, state that remote attendance is not addressed and decline to decide whether the proposed meeting is quorate. It is wrong if it automatically counts the video participant and declares a quorum of four, or automatically excludes that participant and declares only three without acknowledging the missing remote-attendance rule.

**The answer to mark:**

The documents don't say, so I can't give a yes or no. The rules don't specify whether trustees may attend remotely (hall-trustees.md, section 6.3). Nothing in hall-changes.md covers quorum or remote attendance either. That leaves open whether the video-call trustee counts towards quorum.

The outcome depends on that point:
- **If a remote trustee counts:** four trustees are present, which meets the quorum of four (hall-trustees.md, section 4.2), so the meeting would be quorate.
- **If a remote trustee does not count:** only three are present, which is below the quorum of four, so the meeting would not be quorate.

## Answer 9303

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: A registered village group has exactly nine local members and supplies that count when booking the Main Hall for two paid hours on 15 October 2025. The organiser requests the village group discount because the group is registered locally. Does it qualify, and what is the room-hire charge without the discount?

**Expected answer:** No, the group does not qualify for the village group discount because the rules require at least ten local members. Being registered is not sufficient when the group has only nine. On 15 October 2025, the Main Hall costs £24 per hour, so two paid hours cost £48 without a discount.

**Correct requires:** The answer must deny the discount because nine is below the ten-member minimum and give £48 as the room charge. It is wrong if it applies the original 20% discount and charges £38.40, applies the archived eight-member qualification and 15% discount to charge £40.80 at the governing hourly rate, or uses the later £28 hourly rate and charges £56.

**The answer to mark:**

**Does it qualify?** No. Section 3.2 of `hall-fees.md` says a registered village group needs at least ten local members, and this group has nine. Being registered locally isn't enough on its own. The 25% rate in `hall-changes.md` doesn't help either. It only changes the percentage in section 3.1, and it takes effect on 1 February 2026, after this event.

**Room-hire charge without the discount:** £48.
- The Main Hall rate is £24 per hour (section 2.1). The £28 rate only applies to events from 1 November 2025, so it doesn't apply to 15 October 2025.
- Two paid hours is the minimum booking (section 2.4), so 2 × £24 = £48.

This is room hire only. A kitchen (£8), equipment, and the £30 booking deposit would be separate, and the deposit counts towards the total.

## Answer 6506

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: An organiser plans a children's event in the Meeting Room on 19 October 2025. There will be 16 children aged 8 to 11 and five adults, including organisers. The organiser wants to know whether the attendance is permitted and whether the children may be left without adult supervision while the adults prepare another room. What do the rules require?

**Expected answer:** The attendance is permitted because the Meeting Room capacity is 24 people and the total is 21, including the adults and children. However, children under 12 must remain under adult supervision. The organiser therefore cannot leave the children unsupervised while the adults prepare another room.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give a total attendance of 21 against the capacity of 24, permit the attendance and require continuing adult supervision. It is wrong if it rejects the event by counting children and adults against separate capacity allowances, counts only the five adults, or says that being below capacity permits the 16 children to remain unsupervised.

**The answer to mark:**

**Attendance: permitted.**
- The Meeting Room holds a maximum of 24 people (hall-bookings.md 4.2).
- Capacity counts organisers and children (4.4).
- 16 children plus 5 adults is 21, which is 3 under the limit.
- The hirer must count attendees when the event begins (hall-safety.md 3.2).

**Leaving the children unsupervised: not permitted.**
- Children under 12 must remain under adult supervision (hall-safety.md 3.3). All the children are 8 to 11, so the rule covers every one of them. There is no exception for short periods or for adults working in another room.
- Adults can still prepare the other room, but at least one adult must stay with the children. With five adults, that is workable.
- At least one responsible adult must also stay near the main entrance (3.4). The same adult can do both jobs if they can supervise the children from that position. If not, you need two adults on duty.
- The hirer must stay on site for the whole event (hall-conduct.md 1.3).
- Keep corridors and access routes clear while the room is being prepared (hall-safety.md 3.5).
- A serious breach can lead to the event being ended immediately, with no refund of room hire (hall-conduct.md 6.1 and 6.2).

**Other points**
- 19 October 2025 is a Sunday, so the hall closes at 21:00 and amplified music must stop by 21:00 (hall-bookings.md 3.3, hall-conduct.md 4.2).
- None of the entries in hall-changes.md alter these rules for that date.
- Today is 12 October 2026, so that date has already passed. If you meant a 2026 date, the answer is the same, because none of the changes touch capacity or supervision. The Sunday hours would also be the same if the new date is a Sunday. Check the weekday if it isn't.

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: An organiser plans a children's event in the Meeting Room on 19 October 2025. There will be 16 children aged 8 to 11 and five adults, including organisers. The organiser wants to know whether the attendance is permitted and whether the children may be left without adult supervision while the adults prepare another room. What do the rules require?

**Expected answer:** The attendance is permitted because the Meeting Room capacity is 24 people and the total is 21, including the adults and children. However, children under 12 must remain under adult supervision. The organiser therefore cannot leave the children unsupervised while the adults prepare another room.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give a total attendance of 21 against the capacity of 24, permit the attendance and require continuing adult supervision. It is wrong if it rejects the event by counting children and adults against separate capacity allowances, counts only the five adults, or says that being below capacity permits the 16 children to remain unsupervised.

**Attendance: not permitted as planned.**

- The Meeting Room holds a maximum of 24 people (hall-bookings.md 4.2).
- Capacity includes organisers, performers and children (4.4).
- 16 children plus 5 adults is 21 people, which is under the limit. So the attendance is permitted.
- The hirer must count attendees when the event begins (hall-safety.md 3.2). Fire safety requirements also apply (hall-bookings.md 4.5).

**Supervision: the children may not be left unsupervised.**

- At least one responsible adult must also stay near the main entrance (3.4).
- The adults can prepare another room only if enough of them stay with the children. Another adult, or the same one if the Meeting Room is near the entrance, must cover the entrance.
- The named hirer is responsible for the whole booking and must remain on site throughout (hall-conduct.md 1.2, 1.3, and hall-bookings.md 1.3).
- The hirer must also explain the emergency exits to the organisers (hall-safety.md 1.3).

**Changes register:** 19 October 2025 is before every effective date in hall-changes.md, and none of those changes touch capacity or supervision anyway. The original wording applies.

**Correction to my first line:** I wrote "not permitted as planned" for attendance, but the figures show it is permitted. The attendance is fine. Only leaving the children unsupervised is not allowed.

## Answer 6153

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: At a screening on 14 November 2025, a visitor makes a serious threat against another attendee. The visitor has not previously been warned about their behaviour. Must the duty volunteer issue a warning before requiring the visitor to leave?

**Expected answer:** No. Serious threats can result in immediate removal without a warning. Although the ordinary disruption procedure allows a volunteer to ask someone to stop and then require them to leave if they refuse, the serious-threat exception permits immediate removal.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say that no prior warning is required for a serious threat and distinguish that exception from the ordinary stop-and-refuse procedure. It is wrong if it insists that the visitor must first receive a warning or refuse an instruction before removal is permitted.

**The answer to mark:**

**No.** Under section 2.3 of the conduct rules (`film-conduct.md`), "Serious threats may result in immediate removal without a warning." The volunteer can require the visitor to leave straight away, even though the visitor has had no earlier warning.

- **Threats are prohibited.** Section 1.2 prohibits threats, and section 1.3 requires visitors to follow reasonable volunteer instructions.
- **Removal is discretionary.** The rule says "may", so the volunteer can remove the visitor immediately but isn't forced to. The warning route in sections 2.1 and 2.2 (ask them to stop, then require them to leave if they refuse) is for ordinary disruption.
- **The volunteer must record the incident.** Section 2.4 requires this within 48 hours, which for 14 November 2025 means by 16 November.
- **A refund isn't automatic.** Section 5.3 says removal doesn't automatically create a refund right. That is decided under `film-refunds.md`.

The conduct rules took effect on 1 April 2025, so they applied on 14 November 2025. `film-changes.md` has no amendments to the conduct rules.

## Answer 1373

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: Two adults have accepted membership applications. The treasurer receives the first person's £24 payment on 30 June 2025 and the second person's payment on 2 July 2025. How much should each person pay for annual adult membership?

**Expected answer:** The first person pays £24 because the treasurer received payment before 1 July 2025. The second person pays £28 because the revised adult membership fee took effect on 1 July 2025. The payment receipt date, not the application acceptance date, determines which fee applies.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give £24 for 30 June 2025 and £28 for 2 July 2025, explaining that the payment date controls. It is wrong if it charges both £24 using the original fee schedule, both £28 using the latest rate without considering dates, or £20 using the archived schedule.

**The answer to mark:**

Under the Fenwray Film Society rules, the first person pays **£24** and the second person pays **£28**.

- **Base fee:** Annual adult membership is £24 (`film-fees.md` §1.1).
- **Fee change:** The committee's decision of 16 June 2025 raised the fee to £28 from 1 July 2025 (`film-changes.md` §1.2). The date the payment is received sets the fee, and payments received before 1 July keep the old fee (§1.3–1.4).
- **First person:** The treasurer received their payment on 30 June 2025, so the old £24 fee applies.
- **Second person:** The treasurer received their payment on 2 July 2025, so the new £28 fee applies.

The date of acceptance doesn't matter. Payment is only due after acceptance (`film-membership.md` §2.5), but the fee depends only on when payment is received.

## Answer 2856

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: An organiser plans a children's event in the Meeting Room on 19 October 2025. There will be 16 children aged 8 to 11 and five adults, including organisers. The organiser wants to know whether the attendance is permitted and whether the children may be left without adult supervision while the adults prepare another room. What do the rules require?

**Expected answer:** The attendance is permitted because the Meeting Room capacity is 24 people and the total is 21, including the adults and children. However, children under 12 must remain under adult supervision. The organiser therefore cannot leave the children unsupervised while the adults prepare another room.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give a total attendance of 21 against the capacity of 24, permit the attendance and require continuing adult supervision. It is wrong if it rejects the event by counting children and adults against separate capacity allowances, counts only the five adults, or says that being below capacity permits the 16 children to remain unsupervised.

**The answer to mark:**

**Attendance: permitted.** There are 16 children and 5 adults, so 21 people in total. The Meeting Room maximum is 24 (hall-bookings.md 4.2), and that figure includes organisers and children (4.4). The hirer must count attendees when the event begins (hall-safety.md 3.2).

**Leaving the children unsupervised: not permitted.**
- Children under 12 must remain under adult supervision (hall-safety.md 3.3). Children aged 8 to 11 are all under 12, so the rule covers every child in the group. The rules allow no exception for short periods, such as while the adults prepare another room.
- At least one responsible adult must also remain near the main entrance (hall-safety.md 3.4). Whether that adult can be the one supervising the children depends on where the Meeting Room is relative to the entrance, and the rules don't say.
- The hirer must stay on site throughout the event (hall-conduct.md 1.3).

With five adults, some can prepare the other room as long as at least one stays with the children. If that adult is not near the main entrance, a second adult must cover the entrance.

**Other points to check:**
- 19 October 2025 is a Sunday, so the event and any setup and clearing must finish by 21:00 (hall-bookings.md 3.3–3.5).
- The other room must also be booked, and it must be within booked hours. Setup must take place within booked hours (3.5).
- The hirer must explain the emergency exits to the organisers (hall-safety.md 1.3).
- None of the committee changes in hall-changes.md alter these sections.
- The date is in the past relative to today (12 October 2026). If you meant 2025 as the event year, this answer applies as of that date. If the event is actually upcoming, check the date again.

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: At a screening on 14 November 2025, a visitor makes a serious threat against another attendee. The visitor has not previously been warned about their behaviour. Must the duty volunteer issue a warning before requiring the visitor to leave?
