**Expected answer:** The first person pays £24 because the treasurer received payment before 1 July 2025. The second person pays £28 because the revised adult membership fee took effect on 1 July 2025. The payment receipt date, not the application acceptance date, determines which fee applies.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give £24 for 30 June 2025 and £28 for 2 July 2025, explaining that the payment date controls. It is wrong if it charges both £24 using the original fee schedule, both £28 using the latest rate without considering dates, or £20 using the archived schedule.

- Section 1.1 of film-fees.md sets annual adult membership at £24.
- A committee decision of 16 June 2025 raised it to £28 from 1 July 2025. Payments received before 1 July 2025 keep the old fee (film-changes.md §1.2–1.4).

**Expected answer:** The first person pays £24 because the treasurer received payment before 1 July 2025. The second person pays £28 because the revised adult membership fee took effect on 1 July 2025. The payment receipt date, not the application acceptance date, determines which fee applies.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give £24 for 30 June 2025 and £28 for 2 July 2025, explaining that the payment date controls. It is wrong if it charges both £24 using the original fee schedule, both £28 using the latest rate without considering dates, or £20 using the archived schedule.

**The answer to mark:**

The first person pays £24 and the second pays £28.

- **First person:** The payment was received on 30 June 2025, before 1 July 2025, so the old £24 fee applies (film-changes.md, 1.4; film-fees.md, 1.1).
- **Second person:** The payment was received on 2 July 2025, so the new £28 fee applies. The date the payment is received determines the fee (film-changes.md, 1.2 and 1.3).

Both dates are the receipt dates given in the question. A payment counts as received when cash is handed over or a transfer clears (film-fees.md, 4.5). If the second person's payment was a bank transfer that cleared on a different date from 2 July, the answer could change.

## Answer 3735

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: Two adults have accepted membership applications. The treasurer receives the first person's £24 payment on 30 June 2025 and the second person's payment on 2 July 2025. How much should each person pay for annual adult membership?

**Expected answer:** The first person pays £24 because the treasurer received payment before 1 July 2025. The second person pays £28 because the revised adult membership fee took effect on 1 July 2025. The payment receipt date, not the application acceptance date, determines which fee applies.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give £24 for 30 June 2025 and £28 for 2 July 2025, explaining that the payment date controls. It is wrong if it charges both £24 using the original fee schedule, both £28 using the latest rate without considering dates, or £20 using the archived schedule.

**The answer to mark:**

The first person pays £24 and the second pays £28.

The fee depends on the date the payment is received. Payments received before 1 July 2025 keep the old £24 fee, and from 1 July 2025 the fee is £28 (film-changes.md, 1.2–1.4). The first payment was received on 30 June 2025, so £24 applies. The second was received on 2 July 2025, so £28 applies.

## Answer 7572

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: A band is hired for a Saturday evening event on 4 April 2026. Their contract says amplified music will continue until 21:45. The hirer points out that the general hall rules allow Friday and Saturday amplified music until 22:00. May the band play until 21:45?

**Expected answer:** No. From 1 April 2026, amplified music on Friday and Saturday must stop by 21:30. Because the music is being played on 4 April 2026, the revised cutoff applies, and playing until 21:45 would exceed it by 15 minutes.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say no, give 21:30 as the controlling cutoff and identify the proposed 15-minute overrun. It is wrong if it permits 21:45 based on the original 22:00 cutoff, or treats the building's 23:00 Saturday closing time as permission for amplified music until 23:00.

**The answer to mark:**

**No. The band may not play until 21:45. Amplified music must stop by 21:30.**

The original rule, `hall-conduct.md` §4.1, set a 22:00 cut-off on Fridays and Saturdays. The hirer is relying on that wording. It was replaced by `hall-changes.md` §5, a decision made on 20 March 2026 and effective 1 April 2026. Under §5.2, Friday and Saturday amplified music must stop by 21:30. Under §5.3, the controlling date is the date the music is played. That date is 4 April 2026, which is after the 1 April effective date, so the 21:30 rule applies.

The contract's 21:45 time doesn't change this, because the hall's rule sets the limit.

## Answer 7107

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: At a screening on 14 November 2025, a visitor makes a serious threat against another attendee. The visitor has not previously been warned about their behaviour. Must the duty volunteer issue a warning before requiring the visitor to leave?

**Expected answer:** No. Serious threats can result in immediate removal without a warning. Although the ordinary disruption procedure allows a volunteer to ask someone to stop and then require them to leave if they refuse, the serious-threat exception permits immediate removal.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say that no prior warning is required for a serious threat and distinguish that exception from the ordinary stop-and-refuse procedure. It is wrong if it insists that the visitor must first receive a warning or refuse an instruction before removal is permitted.

**The answer to mark:**

No. A warning isn't required. Serious threats may result in immediate removal without a warning (film-conduct.md, section 2.3). No committee change affects that section, so the rule in force on 14 November 2025 is the one that applies.

## Answer 3341

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: An organiser plans a children's event in the Meeting Room on 19 October 2025. There will be 16 children aged 8 to 11 and five adults, including organisers. The organiser wants to know whether the attendance is permitted and whether the children may be left without adult supervision while the adults prepare another room. What do the rules require?

**Expected answer:** The attendance is permitted because the Meeting Room capacity is 24 people and the total is 21, including the adults and children. However, children under 12 must remain under adult supervision. The organiser therefore cannot leave the children unsupervised while the adults prepare another room.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give a total attendance of 21 against the capacity of 24, permit the attendance and require continuing adult supervision. It is wrong if it rejects the event by counting children and adults against separate capacity allowances, counts only the five adults, or says that being below capacity permits the 16 children to remain unsupervised.

**The answer to mark:**

The attendance is permitted, and the children may not be left without adult supervision.

- **Attendance:** 16 children plus 5 adults is 21 people. The Meeting Room maximum is 24, and that figure includes organisers and children (hall-bookings.md, sections 4.2 and 4.4). No changes entry replaces these sections.
- **Supervision:** Children under 12 must remain under adult supervision (hall-safety.md, section 3.3). All the children are aged 8 to 11, so they must stay supervised while the other adults prepare the other room. Section 3.3 doesn't say how many adults are needed or how close they must be, so I can't say what number or position would satisfy it.

## Answer 6066

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A screening is advertised to begin at 19:30 on 18 November 2025. Someone has paid for a reserved seat but has not arrived when the film starts. Until what exact time must the society hold that seat, and when do the doors normally open?

**Expected answer:** The reserved seat must be held until 19:40, which is 10 minutes after the advertised 19:30 start. Doors normally open at 19:00, which is 30 minutes before the advertised start. The seat-holding deadline is not the same as the door-opening time.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give both 19:40 for the reserved-seat deadline and 19:00 for door opening. It is wrong if it gives 19:30 as the seat deadline, 19:00 as both times, or 20:00 by adding the 30-minute door-opening interval after the start.

**The answer to mark:**

The society must hold the seat until **19:40**, and doors normally open at **19:00**.

- **Seat held:** Reserved seats are held until 10 minutes after the advertised start. The start is 19:30, so the hold runs to 19:40 (film-screenings.md, section 3.4).
- **Doors:** Doors open 30 minutes before the advertised start, which is 19:00 (film-screenings.md, section 3.3).

No entry in film-changes.md replaces sections 3.3 or 3.4. The only change to film-screenings.md is to section 3.1, the maximum audience.

## Answer 1942

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A member aged 35 has an active annual membership but is suspended by the committee from 1 November through 20 November 2025. They arrive at a public screening on 8 November 2025 and ask to pay the usual member admission price. Are they entitled to the discount, and what admission price applies instead?

**Expected answer:** No, the suspended member is not entitled to the member discount during the suspension. Suspended members cannot claim member prices, even when their annual membership would otherwise be active. The standard admission price on 8 November 2025 is £7 rather than the £4 active-member price.

**Correct requires:** The answer must deny the discount and give £7 as the applicable standard admission charge, contrasting it with the £4 member price. It is wrong if it charges £4 simply because the annual membership has not expired, £5 by confusing the child ticket band or later member rate, or says suspension permanently ends the membership.

**The answer to mark:**

**No, they aren't entitled to the discount. They pay the standard admission price of £7.**

- **Suspension:** Their annual membership is still active, but the suspension covers 8 November 2025 (1–20 November). Membership rules s5.4 (`film-membership.md`) say a suspended member "cannot vote, book equipment or claim member prices." The £4 member rate in film-fees s2.2 is therefore unavailable to them that day.
- **Price that applies:** Standard admission is £7 per screening (`film-fees.md` s2.1). The child rates in s2.3 and s2.4 don't apply to someone aged 35. The committee's change register has no amendment to s2.1.
- **Why the member rate isn't £5:** The change register raises the member price to £5 only for screenings from 1 February 2026. It doesn't affect this 8 November 2025 screening, so it wouldn't matter here even if they were entitled to a member price.

The suspension doesn't end their membership, and it doesn't extend the membership period. After 20 November 2025 they can claim member prices again.

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: Four trustees want to attend a committee meeting on 15 November 2026, but one proposes joining by video call from home while the other three attend in person. Do the rules allow a remote trustee to count towards the required quorum, and would that make this meeting quorate?

**Expected answer:** The papers do not say whether trustees may attend remotely or whether remote attendance counts towards the quorum. They require four trustees for a quorum but explicitly leave remote attendance unspecified. Consequently, the papers alone cannot establish whether three trustees attending in person plus one joining by video call would constitute a valid quorum.

**Correct requires:** The answer must identify four as the quorum, state that remote attendance is not addressed and decline to decide whether the proposed meeting is quorate. It is wrong if it automatically counts the video participant and declares a quorum of four, or automatically excludes that participant and declares only three without acknowledging the missing remote-attendance rule.

**The answer to mark:**

The rules don't say, so I can't confirm the meeting would be quorate.

Four trustees form a quorum (hall-trustees.md, section 4.2). The rules do not specify whether trustees may attend remotely (hall-trustees.md, section 6.3), so they neither allow nor bar a video attendee from counting.

- **Remote trustee counts:** all four count and the meeting is quorate.
- **Remote trustee doesn't count:** only three are present, so the meeting is not quorate.

The committee would need to decide which applies before 15 November 2026. Otherwise, a fourth trustee would need to attend in person.

## Answer 7563

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: At a screening on 14 November 2025, a visitor makes a serious threat against another attendee. The visitor has not previously been warned about their behaviour. Must the duty volunteer issue a warning before requiring the visitor to leave?

**Expected answer:** No. Serious threats can result in immediate removal without a warning. Although the ordinary disruption procedure allows a volunteer to ask someone to stop and then require them to leave if they refuse, the serious-threat exception permits immediate removal.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say that no prior warning is required for a serious threat and distinguish that exception from the ordinary stop-and-refuse procedure. It is wrong if it insists that the visitor must first receive a warning or refuse an instruction before removal is permitted.

**The answer to mark:**

**No. The rules don't require a warning first in this case.**

- Section 2.3 of `film-conduct.md` says: "Serious threats may result in immediate removal without a warning." A serious threat against another attendee falls under that. The volunteer can remove the visitor straight away, even though the visitor has had no earlier warning.
- The warning route is in sections 2.1 and 2.2. A duty volunteer "may ask someone to stop disruptive behaviour", and a person "who refuses may be required to leave". That is the ordinary route for disruption. Section 2.3 sets it aside for serious threats.
- Section 2.3 uses "may". It lets the volunteer remove the visitor immediately, but it doesn't oblige them to. The rules don't say what counts as a "serious" threat. Section 1.2 prohibits threats in general, and section 1.3 requires visitors to follow reasonable instructions from duty volunteers.
- After the removal, the volunteer must record the incident within 48 hours (2.4). The removal does not by itself end the person's society membership (2.5). It also does not automatically give them a right to a refund (5.3). Refund eligibility is decided under `film-refunds.md` (5.4).

The date doesn't change this answer. `film-conduct.md` was effective from 1 April 2025, so it applied on 14 November 2025. The changes register (`film-changes.md`) has no amendment to the conduct rules.

## Answer 7778

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: An organiser plans a children's event in the Meeting Room on 19 October 2025. There will be 16 children aged 8 to 11 and five adults, including organisers. The organiser wants to know whether the attendance is permitted and whether the children may be left without adult supervision while the adults prepare another room. What do the rules require?

**Expected answer:** The attendance is permitted because the Meeting Room capacity is 24 people and the total is 21, including the adults and children. However, children under 12 must remain under adult supervision. The organiser therefore cannot leave the children unsupervised while the adults prepare another room.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give a total attendance of 21 against the capacity of 24, permit the attendance and require continuing adult supervision. It is wrong if it rejects the event by counting children and adults against separate capacity allowances, counts only the five adults, or says that being below capacity permits the 16 children to remain unsupervised.

**The answer to mark:**

**Attendance: permitted on the numbers given.** 16 children plus 5 adults is 21 people. The Meeting Room maximum is 24, and that figure includes organisers and children (hall-bookings.md §4.2 and §4.4). No changes entry replaces §4. The 21 is within the 24 limit as long as no one else is present. hall-safety.md §3.1 sends capacity questions to this section, and §3.2 requires the hirer to count attendees when the event begins.

**Leaving the children unsupervised: not permitted.** The children are 8 to 11, so they are under 12. Children under 12 must remain under adult supervision (hall-safety.md §3.3). The rules do not make an exception for adults preparing another room. No changes entry replaces §3.3.

Separately, at least one responsible adult must remain near the main entrance (hall-safety.md §3.4). The documents don't say whether that adult may also be the one supervising the children.

## Answer 6279

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A member aged 35 has an active annual membership but is suspended by the committee from 1 November through 20 November 2025. They arrive at a public screening on 8 November 2025 and ask to pay the usual member admission price. Are they entitled to the discount, and what admission price applies instead?

**Expected answer:** No, the suspended member is not entitled to the member discount during the suspension. Suspended members cannot claim member prices, even when their annual membership would otherwise be active. The standard admission price on 8 November 2025 is £7 rather than the £4 active-member price.

**Correct requires:** The answer must deny the discount and give £7 as the applicable standard admission charge, contrasting it with the £4 member price. It is wrong if it charges £4 simply because the annual membership has not expired, £5 by confusing the child ticket band or later member rate, or says suspension permanently ends the membership.

**The answer to mark:**

**No, they aren't entitled to the member discount. They pay the standard admission price of £7.**

- **No member price while suspended.** Membership Rules 5.4 (`film-membership.md`) says a suspended member "cannot vote, book equipment or claim member prices." Conduct Rules 4.4 (`film-conduct.md`) sends the question of rights during suspension to that same rule.
- **The suspension covers the date.** It runs 1–20 November 2025, so 8 November falls inside it. The 20 days is within the 30-day maximum in Conduct Rules 4.1.
- **Active membership doesn't change this.** Fees Rule 2.2 gives £4 to "members with active membership." The membership is still on the books, but 5.4 removes the member-price right during the suspension.
- **Standard admission applies.** Fees Rule 2.1 sets it at £7 per screening. The member's age of 35 doesn't bring in any other category. The reduced prices in 2.3 and 2.4 are only for children under 16.

The member can still attend if they pay £7 and the screening isn't full. Membership Rules 4.4 says membership doesn't guarantee admission, and Screening Rules 3.6 refuses entry once capacity is reached. From 21 November, if the suspension has ended, the £4 price is available again.

- The controlling date is the date the music is played (5.3). That date is Saturday 4 April 2026, which is after the 1 April 2026 effective date. So the 21:30 cutoff applies, and the 22:00 wording no longer does.
- The documents don't say a contract can override the cutoff, so the band's 21:45 finish is 15 minutes past the limit. Section 4.5 of the conduct rules also lets the booking officer require immediate noise reduction.

## Answer 3797

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: Four trustees want to attend a committee meeting on 15 November 2026, but one proposes joining by video call from home while the other three attend in person. Do the rules allow a remote trustee to count towards the required quorum, and would that make this meeting quorate?

**Expected answer:** The papers do not say whether trustees may attend remotely or whether remote attendance counts towards the quorum. They require four trustees for a quorum but explicitly leave remote attendance unspecified. Consequently, the papers alone cannot establish whether three trustees attending in person plus one joining by video call would constitute a valid quorum.

**Correct requires:** The answer must identify four as the quorum, state that remote attendance is not addressed and decline to decide whether the proposed meeting is quorate. It is wrong if it automatically counts the video participant and declares a quorum of four, or automatically excludes that participant and declares only three without acknowledging the missing remote-attendance rule.

**The answer to mark:**

**The rules don't say whether a remote trustee counts towards quorum, so I can't confirm that this meeting would be quorate.**

- **Quorum:** Section 4.2 of `hall-trustees.md` says "Four trustees form a quorum." It doesn't say whether the four must be physically present.
- **Remote attendance:** Section 6.3 says the rules "do not specify whether trustees may attend remotely." So nothing allows a video-call trustee to count, and nothing forbids it.
- **Changes document:** `hall-changes.md` has no entries on quorum, trustees or remote attendance. It doesn't alter this position for 15 November 2026.

**Applying this to your meeting:** Three trustees will be in person and one on video, so the meeting reaches four only if the remote trustee counts. The rules leave that open. If the remote trustee doesn't count, only three are present and the meeting isn't quorate.

**Options:**
- Get the remote trustee to attend in person, so four are physically present and the meeting is quorate on any reading.
- Add a fifth trustee in person, so quorum is met without relying on the remote attendee.
- Ask the committee to decide, ideally at an earlier quorate meeting, whether remote attendance counts. Section 6.3 leaves a gap, so this would need to be settled formally.

- **The £2 price list:** That figure comes from the archived fee schedule (`film-fees-archive.md`, section 2.3). It applied only from 1 February to 28 February 2025. The archive says it "must not be used for current charges."
- **Later changes:** The committee changes register has no amendment to the child ticket price. Its only ticket-price change is the member admission rise to £5 from 1 February 2026, which doesn't affect children.

The volunteer's price list is out of date.

## Answer 5021

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A screening is advertised to begin at 19:30 on 18 November 2025. Someone has paid for a reserved seat but has not arrived when the film starts. Until what exact time must the society hold that seat, and when do the doors normally open?

**Expected answer:** The reserved seat must be held until 19:40, which is 10 minutes after the advertised 19:30 start. Doors normally open at 19:00, which is 30 minutes before the advertised start. The seat-holding deadline is not the same as the door-opening time.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give both 19:40 for the reserved-seat deadline and 19:00 for door opening. It is wrong if it gives 19:30 as the seat deadline, 19:00 as both times, or 20:00 by adding the 30-minute door-opening interval after the start.

**The answer to mark:**

Under the Fenwray Film Society screening rules (`film-screenings.md`), for a screening advertised at 19:30 on 18 November 2025:

- **Seat held until 19:40.** Section 3.4 says reserved seats are held until 10 minutes after the advertised start.
- **Doors normally open at 19:00.** Section 3.3 says doors open 30 minutes before the advertised start time.

The committee changes register (`film-changes.md`) amends section 3.1 (maximum audience) but not sections 3.3 or 3.4, so those two rules are unchanged for this date.

The rules don't say what happens to the seat after 19:40. Section 3.5 says late arrivals may enter only during a suitable break, but it doesn't say whether a seat is still reserved for them.

## Answer 5575

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: A band is hired for a Saturday evening event on 4 April 2026. Their contract says amplified music will continue until 21:45. The hirer points out that the general hall rules allow Friday and Saturday amplified music until 22:00. May the band play until 21:45?

**Expected answer:** No. From 1 April 2026, amplified music on Friday and Saturday must stop by 21:30. Because the music is being played on 4 April 2026, the revised cutoff applies, and playing until 21:45 would exceed it by 15 minutes.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say no, give 21:30 as the controlling cutoff and identify the proposed 15-minute overrun. It is wrong if it permits 21:45 based on the original 22:00 cutoff, or treats the building's 23:00 Saturday closing time as permission for amplified music until 23:00.

**The answer to mark:**

**No.** The band may not play until 21:45. Amplified music on Saturday 4 April 2026 must stop by 21:30.

- **Rule that decides it:** hall-changes.md section 5 replaces hall-conduct.md section 4.1. It took effect on 1 April 2026 and says Friday and Saturday amplified music must stop by 21:30 (5.2). The controlling date is the date the music is played (5.3). That date is 4 April 2026, which is after the effective date, so the replacement applies.
- **The hirer's point:** the 22:00 cutoff the hirer cites is the original wording of hall-conduct.md 4.1. That wording no longer applies to music played on or after 1 April 2026.

The documents say nothing that lets a band contract override the cutoff.

## Answer 7375

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: Four trustees want to attend a committee meeting on 15 November 2026, but one proposes joining by video call from home while the other three attend in person. Do the rules allow a remote trustee to count towards the required quorum, and would that make this meeting quorate?

**Expected answer:** The papers do not say whether trustees may attend remotely or whether remote attendance counts towards the quorum. They require four trustees for a quorum but explicitly leave remote attendance unspecified. Consequently, the papers alone cannot establish whether three trustees attending in person plus one joining by video call would constitute a valid quorum.

**Correct requires:** The answer must identify four as the quorum, state that remote attendance is not addressed and decline to decide whether the proposed meeting is quorate. It is wrong if it automatically counts the video participant and declares a quorum of four, or automatically excludes that participant and declares only three without acknowledging the missing remote-attendance rule.

**The answer to mark:**

The rules don't say. Four trustees form a quorum (hall-trustees.md, section 4.2), but the rules "do not specify whether trustees may attend remotely" (hall-trustees.md, section 6.3). They don't say whether a trustee on a video call counts as present.

The meeting is quorate if the remote trustee counts as present, because that makes four. If the remote trustee doesn't count, only three are present and the meeting isn't quorate under section 4.2. The documents don't settle which applies.

## Answer 5897

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: An organiser plans a children's event in the Meeting Room on 19 October 2025. **Attendance:** Not permitted. The event has 16 children and 5 adults, so 21 people, and the Meeting Room maximum is 24. Separately, at least one responsible adult must remain near the main entrance (hall-safety.md, section 3.4).

No entry in the changes register (hall-changes.md) replaces any of these sections.

## Answer 9476

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: At a screening on 14 November 2025, a visitor makes a serious threat against another attendee. The visitor has not previously been warned about their behaviour. Must the duty volunteer issue a warning before requiring the visitor to leave?

**Expected answer:** No. Serious threats can result in immediate removal without a warning. Although the ordinary disruption procedure allows a volunteer to ask someone to stop and then require them to leave if they refuse, the serious-threat exception permits immediate removal.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say that no prior warning is required for a serious threat and distinguish that exception from the ordinary stop-and-refuse procedure. It is wrong if it insists that the visitor must first receive a warning or refuse an instruction before removal is permitted.

**The answer to mark:**

No. For a serious threat, the duty volunteer does not have to warn the visitor first. Serious threats may result in immediate removal without a warning (film-conduct.md, section 2.3). Threats are prohibited under section 1.2 of the same document.

I checked the changes document (film-changes.md), and it does not replace section 2.3.

**Expected answer:** No, the suspended member is not entitled to the member discount during the suspension. Suspended members cannot claim member prices, even when their annual membership would otherwise be active. The standard admission price on 8 November 2025 is £7 rather than the £4 active-member price.

**Correct requires:** The answer must deny the discount and give £7 as the applicable standard admission charge, contrasting it with the £4 member price. It is wrong if it charges £4 simply because the annual membership has not expired, £5 by confusing the child ticket band or later member rate, or says suspension permanently ends the membership.

**The answer to mark:**

No, they aren't entitled to the member discount. They pay £7.

- **Suspension removes member prices.** The member is suspended from 1 to 20 November 2025, and 8 November falls inside that period. Under `film-membership.md` §5.4, "A suspended member cannot vote, book equipment or claim member prices." This applies even though their annual membership is otherwise active. `film-conduct.md` §4.4 sends membership rights during a suspension to the membership rules.
- **Standard admission applies.** `film-fees.md` §2.1 sets standard admission at £7 per screening. The member rate in §2.2 is only for "members with active membership", and a suspended member can't claim it.
- **The 2026 price change doesn't matter here.** The committee raised the member price from £4 to £5 for screenings from 1 February 2026 (`film-changes.md` §3). This screening is on 8 November 2025, so that change doesn't apply, and the member price wouldn't be reached anyway.

The age of 35 has no effect on the price. Standard admission is £7 for adults.

**The child's ticket costs £3.**

## Answer 3032

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: An organiser plans a children's event in the Meeting Room on 19 October 2025. There will be 16 children aged 8 to 11 and five adults, including organisers. The organiser wants to know whether the attendance is permitted and whether the children may be left without adult supervision while the adults prepare another room. What do the rules require?

**Expected answer:** The attendance is permitted because the Meeting Room capacity is 24 people and the total is 21, including the adults and children. However, children under 12 must remain under adult supervision. The organiser therefore cannot leave the children unsupervised while the adults prepare another room.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give a total attendance of 21 against the capacity of 24, permit the attendance and require continuing adult supervision. It is wrong if it rejects the event by counting children and adults against separate capacity allowances, counts only the five adults, or says that being below capacity permits the 16 children to remain unsupervised.

**The answer to mark:**

**Attendance:** Permitted. The 21 attendees (16 children plus 5 adults) are within the Meeting Room maximum of 24, because capacity counts organisers, performers and children (hall-bookings.md §4.2 and §4.4). No changes entry replaces these sections.

**Leaving the children unsupervised:** Not permitted. All the children are 8 to 11, so they are under 12 and "must remain under adult supervision" (hall-safety.md §3.3). The adults therefore cannot all go off to prepare another room. At least one adult must stay with the children.

hall-safety.md §3.4 also requires at least one responsible adult to remain near the main entrance. The documents don't say whether the same adult can cover both duties.

## Answer 2666

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: Two adults have accepted membership applications. The treasurer receives the first person's £24 payment on 30 June 2025 and the second person's payment on 2 July 2025. How much should each person pay for annual adult membership?

**Expected answer:** The first person pays £24 because the treasurer received payment before 1 July 2025. The second person pays £28 because the revised adult membership fee took effect on 1 July 2025. The payment receipt date, not the application acceptance date, determines which fee applies.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give £24 for 30 June 2025 and £28 for 2 July 2025, explaining that the payment date controls. It is wrong if it charges both £24 using the original fee schedule, both £28 using the latest rate without considering dates, or £20 using the archived schedule.

**The answer to mark:**

The first person should pay **£24** and the second person should pay **£28**.

- **Base rule:** `film-fees.md` §1.1 sets annual adult membership at £24.
- **Change:** `film-changes.md` §1 (decision of 16 June 2025) replaces §1.1. From 1 July 2025 the fee is £28.
- **Deciding date:** The date the payment is received sets the fee (§1.3). Payments received before 1 July 2025 keep the old fee (§1.4). Under `film-fees.md` §4.5, a payment is received when cash is handed over or a transfer clears.

The first person's payment was received on 30 June 2025, before the change, so the old £24 fee applies. The second person's payment was received on 2 July 2025, so the new £28 fee applies. That person's payment is £4 short if they paid £24.

Both applications were accepted, but the acceptance date doesn't matter here. Only the date of payment decides the fee.

## Answer 8636

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: At a screening on 14 November 2025, a visitor makes a serious threat against another attendee. The visitor has not previously been warned about their behaviour. Must the duty volunteer issue a warning before requiring the visitor to leave?

**Expected answer:** No. Serious threats can result in immediate removal without a warning. Although the ordinary disruption procedure allows a volunteer to ask someone to stop and then require them to leave if they refuse, the serious-threat exception permits immediate removal.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say that no prior warning is required for a serious threat and distinguish that exception from the ordinary stop-and-refuse procedure. It is wrong if it insists that the visitor must first receive a warning or refuse an instruction before removal is permitted.

**The answer to mark:**

**No.** The duty volunteer does not have to warn the visitor first. A serious threat may lead to immediate removal without a warning.

The deciding rule is film-conduct.md, section 2.3: "Serious threats may result in immediate removal without a warning." Section 1.2 of the same document prohibits threats. The warning-first route in sections 2.1 and 2.2 (ask the person to stop, then require them to leave if they refuse) is not a precondition for a serious threat.

The changes register (film-changes.md) does not replace any part of film-conduct.md, so section 2.3 applies on 14 November 2025.

**The answer to mark:**

The total equipment charge is **£25**.

- Portable speaker: £10 per booking (hall-fees.md §4.2)
- Folding display boards: 3 sets × £5 per set per booking = £15 (hall-fees.md §4.3)

Of the committee changes in hall-changes.md, none touches the equipment charges. The charges apply to bookings starting on or after 1 April 2025, so a 6 September 2025 booking is covered.

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A screening is advertised to begin at 19:30 on 18 November 2025. Someone has paid for a reserved seat but has not arrived when the film starts. Until what exact time must the society hold that seat, and when do the doors normally open?

**Expected answer:** The reserved seat must be held until 19:40, which is 10 minutes after the advertised 19:30 start. Doors normally open at 19:00, which is 30 minutes before the advertised start. The seat-holding deadline is not the same as the door-opening time.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give both 19:40 for the reserved-seat deadline and 19:00 for door opening. It is wrong if it gives 19:30 as the seat deadline, 19:00 as both times, or 20:00 by adding the 30-minute door-opening interval after the start.

The society must hold the seat until **19:40**, which is 10 minutes after the advertised 19:30 start. Doors normally open at **19:00**, which is 30 minutes before the advertised start.

- **Seat hold:** film-screenings.md, section 3.4 says reserved seats are held until 10 minutes after the advertised start.
- **Doors:** film-screenings.md, section 3.3 says doors open 30 minutes before the advertised start time.

The changes document (film-changes.md) doesn't replace either section. Its only change to film-screenings.md is section 3.1, the maximum audience.

- **Remote attendance:** The rules "do not specify whether trustees may attend remotely" (hall-trustees.md, section 6.3). Nothing in them says a trustee on a video call counts towards the quorum or is excluded from it.

Whether the meeting is quorate on 15 November 2026 depends on whether the remote trustee counts as present:

## Answer 9323

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: At a screening on 14 November 2025, a visitor makes a serious threat against another attendee. The visitor has not previously been warned about their behaviour. Must the duty volunteer issue a warning before requiring the visitor to leave?

**Expected answer:** No. Serious threats can result in immediate removal without a warning. Although the ordinary disruption procedure allows a volunteer to ask someone to stop and then require them to leave if they refuse, the serious-threat exception permits immediate removal.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say that no prior warning is required for a serious threat and distinguish that exception from the ordinary stop-and-refuse procedure. It is wrong if it insists that the visitor must first receive a warning or refuse an instruction before removal is permitted.

**The answer to mark:**

**No. The rules don't require a warning first in this case.**

Under `film-conduct.md`, section 2.3, "Serious threats may result in immediate removal without a warning." Section 1.2 prohibits threats. The rules don't say the visitor must have been warned before.

- **Ordinary disruption works differently.** Section 2.1 lets a duty volunteer ask someone to stop. Under 2.2, only a person who refuses may be required to leave. The ask-first step applies there, but 2.3 sets it aside for serious threats.
- **"May" is permissive.** Section 2.3 allows immediate removal. It doesn't require it. The rules don't say how a volunteer should decide between removing the visitor at once and asking them to stop first.
- **Whether this threat counts as "serious"** is not defined in the documents. That is the volunteer's call on the facts, and I can't decide it for them.
- **Date:** The changes register (`film-changes.md`) has no amendments to the conduct rules. The rules adopted 18 March 2025 and effective 1 April 2025 therefore apply on 14 November 2025.

**Afterwards:**
- The duty volunteer must record the incident within 48 hours (2.4).
- Removal doesn't end membership (2.5).
- Removal doesn't automatically create a refund right. Refund eligibility is decided under `film-refunds.md` (5.3 and 5.4).

## Answer 1264

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: Two adults have accepted membership applications. The treasurer receives the first person's £24 payment on 30 June 2025 and the second person's payment on 2 July 2025. How much should each person pay for annual adult membership?

**Expected answer:** The first person pays £24 because the treasurer received payment before 1 July 2025. The second person pays £28 because the revised adult membership fee took effect on 1 July 2025. The payment receipt date, not the application acceptance date, determines which fee applies.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give £24 for 30 June 2025 and £28 for 2 July 2025, explaining that the payment date controls. It is wrong if it charges both £24 using the original fee schedule, both £28 using the latest rate without considering dates, or £20 using the archived schedule.

**The answer to mark:**

Under the Fenwray Film Society rules, the first person pays £24 and the second pays £28.

- **Original fee:** Annual adult membership costs £24 (`film-fees.md` 1.1).
- **Change:** The committee decision of 16 June 2025 raised the fee to £28 from 1 July 2025 (`film-changes.md` 1.1–1.2).
- **What decides the fee:** The date the membership payment is received. Payments received before 1 July 2025 keep the old fee (`film-changes.md` 1.3–1.4). A payment counts as received when cash is handed over or a transfer clears (`film-fees.md` 4.5).
- **First person:** The treasurer received their payment on 30 June 2025, before the change. The fee is £24.
- **Second person:** The treasurer received their payment on 2 July 2025, after the change. The fee is £28.

Acceptance dates don't affect the fee. Payment is only due after an application is accepted (`film-membership.md` 2.5), and the fee depends on when the payment is received.

## Answer 9325

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A member aged 35 has an active annual membership but is suspended by the committee from 1 November through 20 November 2025. They arrive at a public screening on 8 November 2025 and ask to pay the usual member admission price. Are they entitled to the discount, and what admission price applies instead?

**Expected answer:** No, the suspended member is not entitled to the member discount during the suspension. Suspended members cannot claim member prices, even when their annual membership would otherwise be active. The standard admission price on 8 November 2025 is £7 rather than the £4 active-member price.

**Correct requires:** The answer must deny the discount and give £7 as the applicable standard admission charge, contrasting it with the £4 member price. It is wrong if it charges £4 simply because the annual membership has not expired, £5 by confusing the child ticket band or later member rate, or says suspension permanently ends the membership.

**The answer to mark:**

**No, they aren't entitled to the member discount. They pay the standard admission price of £7.**

- **Suspension:** The suspension runs 1–20 November 2025, so 8 November falls inside it. Under `film-membership.md` §5.4, "a suspended member cannot vote, book equipment or claim member prices." Their annual membership is still active, but the suspension removes the right to the member price. `film-conduct.md` §4.4 confirms that membership rights during suspension are governed by the membership rules.
- **Price:** `film-fees.md` §2.1 sets standard admission at £7 per screening. At 35, they qualify for none of the child rates in §2.3 and §2.4.
- **Amendment:** The £5 member rate from the 19 January 2026 decision in `film-changes.md` doesn't apply. It only covers screenings on or after 1 February 2026, and the original £4 member price is the one that would have applied here if they weren't suspended. Neither matters, because the standard £7 applies.

From 21 November 2025, when the suspension ends, they can claim the member price again, assuming the membership is still active.

No. On the documents' wording, the band may not play until 21:45. The cutoff for that evening is 21:30.

- **Controlling date:** this is the date the music is played (5.3). The event is on Saturday 4 April 2026, which is after the 1 April effective date, so the replacement applies.
- **Register:** the changes register says the original conduct document keeps its adopted wording (1.3). That is why the 22:00 figure still appears there. The register says the replacement governs from the effective date (1.1, 1.4).
- **Quick guide:** `hall-quick-guide.md` section 5 also gives 21:30.

The documents don't mention a band contract, and they give no way for a contract to override the hall's cutoff. The contract's 21:45 end time falls 15 minutes after the 21:30 limit.

I can't waive the rule or say what the hall will decide. I can only report that the documents set the cutoff at 21:30 for 4 April 2026.

## Answer 2434

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A member aged 35 has an active annual membership but is suspended by the committee from 1 November through 20 November 2025. They arrive at a public screening on 8 November 2025 and ask to pay the usual member admission price. Are they entitled to the discount, and what admission price applies instead?

**Expected answer:** No, the suspended member is not entitled to the member discount during the suspension. Suspended members cannot claim member prices, even when their annual membership would otherwise be active. The standard admission price on 8 November 2025 is £7 rather than the £4 active-member price.

**Correct requires:** The answer must deny the discount and give £7 as the applicable standard admission charge, contrasting it with the £4 member price. It is wrong if it charges £4 simply because the annual membership has not expired, £5 by confusing the child ticket band or later member rate, or says suspension permanently ends the membership.

**The answer to mark:**

**No, they aren't entitled to the member price. They pay the standard admission of £7.**

- **Suspension removes member prices.** Membership rules 5.4 (`film-membership.md`) say a suspended member "cannot vote, book equipment or claim member prices." Their annual membership is still active, but 8 November 2025 falls inside the suspension (1–20 November 2025). Fees rule 2.2 gives the member price only to "members with active membership", so they don't qualify that day.
- **Standard admission is £7.** Fees rule 2.1 (`film-fees.md`) sets it at £7 per screening. At 35 they don't fall under the child rates in 2.3 and 2.4. No committee decision in `film-changes.md` alters the £7 standard price.
- **The £5 member price doesn't apply either.** The committee raised the member price from £4 to £5, but only for screenings from 1 February 2026. A screening on 8 November 2025 would have been £4 for a member in good standing, and that's moot here.

Once the suspension ends after 20 November 2025, they can claim the member price again.

## Answer 1582

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A screening is advertised to begin at 19:30 on 18 November 2025. Someone has paid for a reserved seat but has not arrived when the film starts. Until what exact time must the society hold that seat, and when do the doors normally open?

**Expected answer:** The reserved seat must be held until 19:40, which is 10 minutes after the advertised 19:30 start. Doors normally open at 19:00, which is 30 minutes before the advertised start. The seat-holding deadline is not the same as the door-opening time.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give both 19:40 for the reserved-seat deadline and 19:00 for door opening. It is wrong if it gives 19:30 as the seat deadline, 19:00 as both times, or 20:00 by adding the 30-minute door-opening interval after the start.

**The answer to mark:**

The society must hold the seat until **19:40**, and doors normally open at **19:00**.

- **Seat hold:** Reserved seats are held until 10 minutes after the advertised start. For a 19:30 start, that is 19:40 (film-screenings.md, section 3.4).
- **Doors:** Doors open 30 minutes before the advertised start, so at 19:00 (film-screenings.md, section 3.3).

The changes document (film-changes.md) replaces only section 3.1 of film-screenings.md, on maximum audience. Sections 3.3 and 3.4 are unaffected.

## Answer 7441

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: Four trustees want to attend a committee meeting on 15 November 2026, but one proposes joining by video call from home while the other three attend in person. Do the rules allow a remote trustee to count towards the required quorum, and would that make this meeting quorate?

**Expected answer:** The papers do not say whether trustees may attend remotely or whether remote attendance counts towards the quorum. They require four trustees for a quorum but explicitly leave remote attendance unspecified. Consequently, the papers alone cannot establish whether three trustees attending in person plus one joining by video call would constitute a valid quorum.

**Correct requires:** The answer must identify four as the quorum, state that remote attendance is not addressed and decline to decide whether the proposed meeting is quorate. It is wrong if it automatically counts the video participant and declares a quorum of four, or automatically excludes that participant and declares only three without acknowledging the missing remote-attendance rule.

**The answer to mark:**

The rules don't say. They set the quorum at four trustees (hall-trustees.md, 4.2) and state that they "do not specify whether trustees may attend remotely" (hall-trustees.md, 6.3).

So whether the meeting is quorate depends on whether the remote trustee counts as attending. If they count, four trustees are present and the meeting is quorate. If they don't, only three are present and it isn't. The rules give no basis for choosing between the two, so the committee would need to decide that first.

## Answer 7238

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: At a screening on 14 November 2025, a visitor makes a serious threat against another attendee. The visitor has not previously been warned about their behaviour. Must the duty volunteer issue a warning before requiring the visitor to leave?

**Expected answer:** No. Serious threats can result in immediate removal without a warning. Although the ordinary disruption procedure allows a volunteer to ask someone to stop and then require them to leave if they refuse, the serious-threat exception permits immediate removal.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say that no prior warning is required for a serious threat and distinguish that exception from the ordinary stop-and-refuse procedure. It is wrong if it insists that the visitor must first receive a warning or refuse an instruction before removal is permitted.

**The answer to mark:**

**No, a prior warning isn't required.** Under section 2.3 of `film-conduct.md`, "Serious threats may result in immediate removal without a warning." Section 1.2 prohibits threats, and the visitor's behaviour falls under it.

- **Warning steps:** Sections 2.1 and 2.2 (ask the person to stop, then require them to leave if they refuse) cover ordinary disruption. They don't apply to a serious threat.
- **Discretion:** The rule says removal "may" happen immediately, so the volunteer can remove the visitor straight away but isn't obliged to. The rules don't define "serious", so the volunteer has to judge that.
- **After removal:** The volunteer must record the incident within 48 hours (2.4). Removal doesn't end membership (2.5) and doesn't create a refund right (5.3).
- **Amendments:** `film-changes.md` doesn't amend the conduct rules, so the 14 November 2025 date makes no difference. The conduct rules were effective from 1 April 2025.

## Answer 5549

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: A band is hired for a Saturday evening event on 4 April 2026. Their contract says amplified music will continue until 21:45. The hirer points out that the general hall rules allow Friday and Saturday amplified music until 22:00. May the band play until 21:45?

**Expected answer:** No. From 1 April 2026, amplified music on Friday and Saturday must stop by 21:30. Because the music is being played on 4 April 2026, the revised cutoff applies, and playing until 21:45 would exceed it by 15 minutes.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say no, give 21:30 as the controlling cutoff and identify the proposed 15-minute overrun. It is wrong if it permits 21:45 based on the original 22:00 cutoff, or treats the building's 23:00 Saturday closing time as permission for amplified music until 23:00.

**The answer to mark:**

No. Amplified music on a Friday or Saturday must stop by 21:30 when the music is played on or after 1 April 2026, so the band cannot play until 21:45 on Saturday 4 April 2026 (hall-changes.md, section 5, replacing hall-conduct.md section 4.1). The 22:00 cutoff the hirer cites is the original wording of section 4.1, and the change replaces it.

## Answer 2571

## Answer 4665

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: Two adults have accepted membership applications. The treasurer receives the first person's £24 payment on 30 June 2025 and the second person's payment on 2 July 2025. How much should each person pay for annual adult membership?

**Expected answer:** The first person pays £24 because the treasurer received payment before 1 July 2025. The second person pays £28 because the revised adult membership fee took effect on 1 July 2025. The payment receipt date, not the application acceptance date, determines which fee applies.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give £24 for 30 June 2025 and £28 for 2 July 2025, explaining that the payment date controls. It is wrong if it charges both £24 using the original fee schedule, both £28 using the latest rate without considering dates, or £20 using the archived schedule.

**The answer to mark:**

**First person: £24. Second person: £28.**

- **Base rule:** `film-fees.md` section 1.1 sets annual adult membership at £24.
- **Change:** `film-changes.md` section 1 (decision of 16 June 2025) replaces that rule. From 1 July 2025 the fee is £28 (1.2).
- **What decides the fee:** The date the payment is received (1.3). Payments received before 1 July 2025 keep the old fee (1.4).
- **First person:** Their payment was received on 30 June 2025, before 1 July, so the old £24 fee applies.
- **Second person:** Their payment was received on 2 July 2025, after the change took effect, so the fee is £28. If they paid only £24, the rules require the full fee before the service is provided (`film-fees.md` 4.3), so £4 would still be due.

The date the application was accepted does not matter. Only the date of receipt does. A payment counts as received when cash is handed over or a bank transfer clears (`film-fees.md` 4.5). I've taken the dates you gave as those receipt dates.

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A parent brings their 9-year-old child to a public screening on 16 August 2025 and accompanies them throughout. A volunteer has found an old price list showing that children under 12 pay £2. What is the correct ticket price for the child?

**Expected answer:** The correct ticket price is £3. The £2 price appears in the archived schedule that was superseded on 1 March 2025. The governing schedule charges accompanied children under 12 £3, and no later change alters that concession.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give £3, noting that the child is under 12 and accompanied by an adult. It is wrong if it gives £2 from the archived schedule, £5 for the 12–15 age group, or £7 for standard admission while overlooking the applicable child concession.

**The answer to mark:**

The child's ticket is **£3**.

Under film-fees.md section 2.3, children aged under 12 pay £3 when accompanied by an adult. The child is 9 and the parent accompanies them throughout, so this applies. The film-changes.md register has no entry that replaces section 2.3. The £2 price list is not part of the governing rules.

## Answer 8196

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: Four trustees want to attend a committee meeting on 15 November 2026, but one proposes joining by video call from home while the other three attend in person. Do the rules allow a remote trustee to count towards the required quorum, and would that make this meeting quorate?

**Expected answer:** The papers do not say whether trustees may attend remotely or whether remote attendance counts towards the quorum. They require four trustees for a quorum but explicitly leave remote attendance unspecified. Consequently, the papers alone cannot establish whether three trustees attending in person plus one joining by video call would constitute a valid quorum.

**Correct requires:** The answer must identify four as the quorum, state that remote attendance is not addressed and decline to decide whether the proposed meeting is quorate. It is wrong if it automatically counts the video participant and declares a quorum of four, or automatically excludes that participant and declares only three without acknowledging the missing remote-attendance rule.

**The answer to mark:**

The rules don't say whether a remote trustee counts towards the quorum, so they don't settle whether this meeting is quorate.

- **Quorum:** Section 4.2 of `hall-trustees.md` says "Four trustees form a quorum." It doesn't say whether a trustee has to be physically present to count.
- **Remote attendance:** Section 6.3 says the rules "do not specify whether trustees may attend remotely." No other document in `reference/` mentions video, remote or virtual attendance.

**Applied to your case:**
- Three trustees would be there in person, which is one short of four.
- The meeting is quorate only if the trustee on video counts. The rules neither allow nor exclude that.
- I can't say how the committee would decide this, and I can't grant permission for it. The rules just don't cover it.

## Answer 6273

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: At a screening on 14 November 2025, a visitor makes a serious threat against another attendee. The visitor has not previously been warned about their behaviour. Must the duty volunteer issue a warning before requiring the visitor to leave?

**Expected answer:** No. Serious threats can result in immediate removal without a warning. Although the ordinary disruption procedure allows a volunteer to ask someone to stop and then require them to leave if they refuse, the serious-threat exception permits immediate removal.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say that no prior warning is required for a serious threat and distinguish that exception from the ordinary stop-and-refuse procedure. It is wrong if it insists that the visitor must first receive a warning or refuse an instruction before removal is permitted.

**The answer to mark:**

No. A serious threat may lead to immediate removal without a warning, so the duty volunteer doesn't have to warn first (film-conduct.md, section 2.3).

## Answer 4429

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: An organiser plans a children's event in the Meeting Room on 19 October 2025. There will be 16 children aged 8 to 11 and five adults, including organisers. The organiser wants to know whether the attendance is permitted and whether the children may be left without adult supervision while the adults prepare another room. What do the rules require?

**Expected answer:** The attendance is permitted because the Meeting Room capacity is 24 people and the total is 21, including the adults and children. However, children under 12 must remain under adult supervision. The organiser therefore cannot leave the children unsupervised while the adults prepare another room.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give a total attendance of 21 against the capacity of 24, permit the attendance and require continuing adult supervision. It is wrong if it rejects the event by counting children and adults against separate capacity allowances, counts only the five adults, or says that being below capacity permits the 16 children to remain unsupervised.

**The answer to mark:**

**Attendance:** Yes, it is permitted. The event has 21 people (16 children and 5 adults), and the Meeting Room maximum is 24, counting organisers and children (hall-bookings.md, sections 4.2 and 4.4).

**Leaving the children unsupervised:** No. All the children are under 12 (aged 8 to 11), and "Children under 12 must remain under adult supervision" (hall-safety.md, section 3.3). The rules make no exception for adults preparing another room.

No changes-register entry replaces any of these sections.

## Answer 2789

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: An organiser plans a children's event in the Meeting Room on 19 October 2025. There will be 16 children aged 8 to 11 and five adults, including organisers. The organiser wants to know whether the attendance is permitted and whether the children may be left without adult supervision while the adults prepare another room. What do the rules require?

**Expected answer:** The attendance is permitted because the Meeting Room capacity is 24 people and the total is 21, including the adults and children. However, children under 12 must remain under adult supervision. The organiser therefore cannot leave the children unsupervised while the adults prepare another room.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give a total attendance of 21 against the capacity of 24, permit the attendance and require continuing adult supervision. It is wrong if it rejects the event by counting children and adults against separate capacity allowances, counts only the five adults, or says that being below capacity permits the 16 children to remain unsupervised.

**The answer to mark:**

**Attendance: permitted.**
- The Meeting Room has a maximum of 24 people (hall-bookings.md 4.2).
- Capacity includes organisers, performers and children (4.4).
- 16 children plus 5 adults is 21, which is within the limit.
- The hirer must count attendees when the event begins (hall-safety.md 3.2).
- The building-wide cap of 100 applies only to combined events (hall-bookings.md 4.3). Nothing in the rules suggests it is relevant here.

**Leaving the children unsupervised: not permitted.**
- Children under 12 must remain under adult supervision (hall-safety.md 3.3). All the children are aged 8 to 11, so the rule covers every one of them.
- The rules make no exception for the adults preparing another room. They set no minimum ratio of adults to children and don't define "supervision". So I can't say how many adults are needed. I can only say the children can't be left without adult supervision.
- At least one responsible adult must also stay near the main entrance (hall-safety.md 3.4). With five adults, someone would need to cover that post and the supervision of the children while the others prepare the room.

**Changes register:** none of the entries in hall-changes.md touch capacity, supervision or the Meeting Room rules. The 19 October 2025 date doesn't alter the answer.

## Answer 5030

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: An organiser plans a children's event in the Meeting Room on 19 October 2025. There will be 16 children aged 8 to 11 and five adults, including organisers. The organiser wants to know whether the attendance is permitted and whether the children may be left without adult supervision while the adults prepare another room. What do the rules require?

**Expected answer:** The attendance is permitted because the Meeting Room capacity is 24 people and the total is 21, including the adults and children. However, children under 12 must remain under adult supervision. The organiser therefore cannot leave the children unsupervised while the adults prepare another room.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give a total attendance of 21 against the capacity of 24, permit the attendance and require continuing adult supervision. It is wrong if it rejects the event by counting children and adults against separate capacity allowances, counts only the five adults, or says that being below capacity permits the 16 children to remain unsupervised.

**The answer to mark:**

**Attendance: permitted.**
- The Meeting Room holds a maximum of 24 people (hall-bookings.md 4.2).
- That limit includes organisers and children (4.4).
- 16 children plus 5 adults is 21 people, which is 3 under the limit.
- The hirer must count attendees when the event begins (hall-safety.md 3.2).
- Nothing in the changes register alters the capacity or supervision rules, so the original wording applies to 19 October 2025.

**Leaving the children unsupervised: not permitted.**
- Children under 12 must remain under adult supervision (hall-safety.md 3.3).
- All 16 children are aged 8 to 11, so the rule covers every one of them.
- The rules don't define "supervision". They don't say whether an adult must be in the room or how many adults are needed per child. They only say the children must remain under adult supervision.
- The rules therefore give no basis for leaving the children with no adult while the adults prepare another room. At least some adults would have to stay with them.

**Related requirements:**
- At least one responsible adult must remain near the main entrance (3.4). Whether that adult can also be the one supervising the children isn't stated.
- Corridors and access routes must remain clear (3.5).
- The named hirer must be an adult aged 18 or over (hall-bookings.md 1.2). The hirer is responsible for the entire booking (1.3) and must explain the emergency exits to organisers (hall-safety.md 1.3).

If the second room is also hired, hall-bookings.md 4.3 caps combined events at 100 people in the building. That is not a concern at 21 people.

## Answer 3497

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: Two adults have accepted membership applications. The treasurer receives the first person's £24 payment on 30 June 2025 and the second person's payment on 2 July 2025. How much should each person pay for annual adult membership?

**Expected answer:** The first person pays £24 because the treasurer received payment before 1 July 2025. The second person pays £28 because the revised adult membership fee took effect on 1 July 2025. The payment receipt date, not the application acceptance date, determines which fee applies.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give £24 for 30 June 2025 and £28 for 2 July 2025, explaining that the payment date controls. It is wrong if it charges both £24 using the original fee schedule, both £28 using the latest rate without considering dates, or £20 using the archived schedule.

**The answer to mark:**

- **First person: £24.** The treasurer received their payment on 30 June 2025, before 1 July 2025, so the old fee applies (film-changes.md §1.4).
- **Second person: £28.** The treasurer received their payment on 2 July 2025, after the increase took effect, so the new fee applies (film-changes.md §1.2).

The 16 June 2025 committee decision replaced section 1.1 of film-fees.md. It raised annual adult membership from £24 to £28 from 1 July 2025. The fee depends on the date the payment is received, not the date the application was accepted (§1.3). Under film-fees.md §4.5, a payment counts as received when cash is handed over or a transfer clears.

The second person is £4 short if they paid £24, and the treasurer needs to collect the difference. Membership fees are paid in full before service (film-fees.md §4.3), and instalments aren't accepted (§4.2).

## Answer 7175

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: A registered village group has exactly nine local members and supplies that count when booking the Main Hall for two paid hours on 15 October 2025. The organiser requests the village group discount because the group is registered locally. Does it qualify, and what is the room-hire charge without the discount?

**Expected answer:** No, the group does not qualify for the village group discount because the rules require at least ten local members. Being registered is not sufficient when the group has only nine. On 15 October 2025, the Main Hall costs £24 per hour, so two paid hours cost £48 without a discount.

**Correct requires:** The answer must deny the discount because nine is below the ten-member minimum and give £48 as the room charge. It is wrong if it applies the original 20% discount and charges £38.40, applies the archived eight-member qualification and 15% discount to charge £40.80 at the governing hourly rate, or uses the later £28 hourly rate and charges £56.

**The answer to mark:**

**No, the group doesn't qualify.** Without the discount, the room-hire charge is **£48**.

- **Discount:** hall-fees.md §3.2 says a registered village group must have at least ten local members. This group has nine, so it falls short. Being registered locally isn't enough on its own.
- **Charge:** hall-fees.md §2.1 sets Main Hall hire at £24 per hour, and §2.4 sets a two-hour minimum. That gives 2 × £24 = £48.
- **Rate:** hall-changes.md §2 raises the Main Hall rate to £28, but only for events on or after 1 November 2025. An event on 15 October 2025 is before that date, so £24 applies.

The £48 covers Main Hall hire only. The question mentions no kitchen, equipment or deposits, so I haven't included any.

## Answer 7533

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: A hirer books the Meeting Room on 6 September 2025 and asks for one portable speaker and three sets of folding display boards. Ignoring room hire and deposits, what is the total equipment charge?

**Expected answer:** The total equipment charge is £25. The portable speaker costs £10 per booking and folding display boards cost £5 per set per booking. Three sets cost £15, so the speaker and boards together cost £10 + £15 = £25.

**Correct requires:** The answer must give £25 and show £10 + (3 × £5). It is wrong if it charges £15 by treating all three sets of boards as one set, £13 by using the archived speaker and board prices as one-time charges, or £20 by charging for only two sets.

**The answer to mark:**

The total equipment charge is **£25**.

- One portable speaker costs £10 per booking (hall-fees.md §4.2).
- Three sets of folding display boards cost £5 per set per booking, so 3 × £5 = £15 (§4.3).

The 6 September 2025 booking falls under these charges because they apply to bookings starting on or after 1 April 2025 (§1.2). The committee changes register (hall-changes.md) doesn't alter any equipment charge.

This assumes the equipment is hired alongside the Meeting Room, as §4.4 requires. It also assumes the booking officer confirms availability (§4.5) and the hirer requests the equipment when booking (hall-bookings.md §5.3). The 20% village-group discount wouldn't reduce this figure either way, because it doesn't apply to equipment (§3.4).

## Answer 1995

**Question:** Under the Fenwray Film Society rules: A member aged 35 has an active annual membership but is suspended by the committee from 1 November through 20 November 2025. They arrive at a public screening on 8 November 2025 and ask to pay the usual member admission price. Are they entitled to the discount, and what admission price applies instead?

**Expected answer:** No, the suspended member is not entitled to the member discount during the suspension. Suspended members cannot claim member prices, even when their annual membership would otherwise be active. The standard admission price on 8 November 2025 is £7 rather than the £4 active-member price.

**Correct requires:** The answer must deny the discount and give £7 as the applicable standard admission charge, contrasting it with the £4 member price. It is wrong if it charges £4 simply because the annual membership has not expired, £5 by confusing the child ticket band or later member rate, or says suspension permanently ends the membership.

**The answer to mark:**

No, they aren't entitled to the member discount. The admission price that applies is the standard £7.

- **Discount:** A suspended member cannot claim member prices (film-membership.md, s5.4). The suspension runs 1–20 November 2025, so 8 November falls inside it. Film-conduct.md s4.4 sends member rights during suspension to film-membership.md.
- **Price:** Standard admission costs £7 per screening (film-fees.md, s2.1). The member price in s2.2 is limited to members with active membership. The reduced rates in s2.3 and s2.4 are for children, and this member is 35.
- **Changes register:** The only change to member admission (film-changes.md, s3) takes effect from 1 February 2026. It doesn't apply to an 8 November 2025 screening and doesn't alter the standard price.

## Answer 5294

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: A band is hired for a Saturday evening event on 4 April 2026. Their contract says amplified music will continue until 21:45. The hirer points out that the general hall rules allow Friday and Saturday amplified music until 22:00. May the band play until 21:45?

**Expected answer:** No. From 1 April 2026, amplified music on Friday and Saturday must stop by 21:30. Because the music is being played on 4 April 2026, the revised cutoff applies, and playing until 21:45 would exceed it by 15 minutes.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say no, give 21:30 as the controlling cutoff and identify the proposed 15-minute overrun. It is wrong if it permits 21:45 based on the original 22:00 cutoff, or treats the building's 23:00 Saturday closing time as permission for amplified music until 23:00.

**The answer to mark:**

No. Section 4.1 of hall-conduct.md (22:00 on Friday and Saturday) was replaced from 1 April 2026. Amplified music on a Friday or Saturday must now stop by 21:30, and the controlling date is the day the music is played (hall-changes.md, section 5). The band plays on Saturday 4 April 2026, which is after the effective date, so 21:30 applies and 21:45 is too late.

## Answer 3460

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: A band is hired for a Saturday evening event on 4 April 2026. Their contract says amplified music will continue until 21:45. The hirer points out that the general hall rules allow Friday and Saturday amplified music until 22:00. May the band play until 21:45?

**Expected answer:** No. From 1 April 2026, amplified music on Friday and Saturday must stop by 21:30. Because the music is being played on 4 April 2026, the revised cutoff applies, and playing until 21:45 would exceed it by 15 minutes.

**Correct requires:** The answer must say no, give 21:30 as the controlling cutoff and identify the proposed 15-minute overrun. It is wrong if it permits 21:45 based on the original 22:00 cutoff, or treats the building's 23:00 Saturday closing time as permission for amplified music until 23:00.

**The answer to mark:**

**No. The band must stop by 21:30, so 21:45 is 15 minutes too late.**

- The hall conduct rules, section 4.1, originally set a 22:00 cutoff for amplified music on Fridays and Saturdays (`reference/hall/hall-conduct.md:30`). That is the rule the hirer is relying on.
- A committee decision made on 20 March 2026 replaced section 4.1 (`reference/hall/hall-changes.md:38-41`). It took effect on 1 April 2026 and says Friday and Saturday amplified music must stop by 21:30.
- That decision is controlled by the date the music is played. The band plays on 4 April 2026, which is after 1 April, so the new 21:30 cutoff applies.
- The hall's quick guide also says 21:30, but it is only a summary.

The 22:00 time the hirer cites is the superseded wording. The band's contract can't override the hall rules, and I found nothing in the rules that lets a hirer or contract extend the cutoff. The band should finish by 21:30. The hall's opening hours still allow guests to stay until 23:00 on a Saturday. Only the amplified music has the earlier cutoff.

## Answer 2031

**Question:** Under the Quillbrook Village Hall rules: A registered village group has exactly nine local members and supplies that count when booking the Main Hall for two paid hours on 15 October 2025. The organiser requests the village group discount because the group is registered locally. Does it qualify, and what is the room-hire charge without the discount?

**Expected answer:** No, the group does not qualify for the village group discount because the rules require at least ten local members. Being registered is not sufficient when the group has only nine. On 15 October 2025, the Main Hall costs £24 per hour, so two paid hours cost £48 without a discount.

**Correct requires:** The answer must deny the discount because nine is below the ten-member minimum and give £48 as the room charge. It is wrong if it applies the original 20% discount and charges £38.40, applies the archived eight-member qualification and 15% discount to charge £40.80 at the governing hourly rate, or uses the later £28 hourly rate and charges £56.

**The answer to mark:**

**Discount: no.** Hall-fees.md §3.2 says a registered village group must have at least ten local members. This group has nine, so it doesn't qualify. Being registered locally isn't enough on its own. The group did supply its count before booking, as §3.3 requires.

**Room-hire charge without the discount: £48.**
- Main Hall is £24 per hour (hall-fees.md §2.1).
- Two paid hours meets the two-hour minimum (§2.4), so 2 × £24 = £48.

The committee changes in hall-changes.md don't alter this. The Main Hall rise to £28 takes effect on 1 November 2025, and the 25% discount on 1 February 2026. Both depend on the first date of the event, which here is 15 October 2025, so the original £24 rate and 20% discount rule apply.

This figure covers room hire only. It leaves out the kitchen (£8 per booking), any equipment, and deposits. The £30 booking deposit is required and counts towards the total hire charge (§5.1, §5.4).
